  ```sh
    --json status,conclusion \
    --jq 'if .[0].status == "completed" and .[0].conclusion == "success" then "yes" else "no" end'
  ```
- Before a prerelease is cut, its tip is checked as a packaged build on
  this machine: `SM_FLAVOR=lab pnpm -C app package` at the tip, then
  `pnpm walks` on that build (app/lab/walks.md: the pages on real git
  through the window), then `node app/scripts/smoke-app.mts "<the lab
  .app>" --serve 9341` with weblab attached to `127.0.0.1:9341` for
  what no walk covers yet (the first run, a terminal), then `smlab nuke
  --yes`, then the cut (app/lab/dev-app.md, "Packaged build, as a test
  flavor"). The release job's own smoke then checks the signed bundle.
- Then, before the tag, the installed previous prerelease on this
  machine (the owner's dogfood, `~/.smv3`, signed in to the dev hub)
  takes the tip through Settings' "Restart to update" and comes back on
  it, signed in. The tip isn't on GitHub until the tag, and the release
  job builds only once a release is published, so the installed app is
  pointed at a local build of it, made as the installed version's `.1`
  (3.0.0-alpha.8.1 over alpha.8, so the tag cut after it is still an
  update): `pnpm -C app pkg set version=<installed>.1` (not committed),
  then `SM_FLAVOR=v3 pnpm -C app package` at the tip with
  `SM_DEVICE_HUB_URL` and `SM_ACCOUNT_CLERK_PUBLISHABLE_KEY` set from
  `gh variable get SM_PREVIEW_DEVICE_HUB_URL` and
  `SM_PREVIEW_ACCOUNT_CLERK_PUBLISHABLE_KEY` and
  `APPLE_SIGNING_IDENTITY` set, then `node app/scripts/smoke-app.mts
  "<the v3 .app>" --update-installed "/Applications/Shigoto no Mori
  (v3).app" ~/.smv3` with the identity set, once the installed app has
  made its first check (about a minute after it starts): `updater.json`
  names the running app only from then, and the step refuses before
  that, leaving nothing behind (its `sm-candidate-*` temp folder is made
  only once every check passed). It offers the build from a
  local release list, quits the app as the menu's Quit does and opens
  it on that list, waits for the staged update, presses the button the
  way the window does
  (`app/scripts/press-restart-to-update.mts`, its loopback call, since a
  packaged build takes no debugger), and passes once the app is back
  on the new version, by its bundle and by its own word in
  `updater.json`, and `smv3 devices` answers signed in. The
  relaunch is on the real feed again. A failure stops the cut.
- Before the rc: a code-quality review pass, a remote-feature pass and
  a security audit, as v2 did (#272, #281, #285, #305).
- `v3.0.0-rc.1`, then one merge into `main` and the `v3.0.0` tag. The
  production hub and the web client deploy with it.

## Steps

Steps 2 and 3 run in parallel in separate threads after step 1. Step
4 needs 3 and the engine's control client from 2. Step 5 needs 4's
protocol. Step 6 needs 4. Anything that adds a feature goes in
step 8, whenever it lands.

### Step 0: groundwork

- [x] Cut `release/v3` and commit this file.
- [x] `EFFECT.md`, the conventions, before any Effect code.
- [x] `PRODUCT.md` names Effect as the house mechanism; `AGENTS.md`
      points at `EFFECT.md` and this file; `app/DESIGN.md` gets the
      views-and-containers rule; the branch's `CLAUDE.md` carries the
      working rules.
- [x] Workspace packages: the pnpm workspace at the root, the catalog,
      the package folders (decision 9). Scaffold only; code moves with
      its step. The root stops forwarding scripts. `desktop` and `web`
      are still `app/` and split off when steps 4 and 6 move their code.
- [x] Vitest for the proofs (decision 10). #501's CI job stays; its
      import-based proof selection is replaced by `vitest related`.
- [x] Tooling: `@effect/tsgo` for typecheck with Effect diagnostics,
      the lint plugin with one rule per file and a test per rule,
      `knip` in CI, `pnpm check`. The scaffolds get a typecheck script
      with their first source file.
      The two rules left until Effect code existed to test them against
      came before the first prerelease: `no-env-or-clock-in-service` (no
      `process.env`, `Date.now()` or `new Date()` in a service module) and
      `effect-fn-service-methods` (every method a service's tag types as
      an effect is `Effect.fn`). The second reads the tag's interface in
      the module, so a sub-maker's methods (`worktrees/listing.ts`) and a
      tag typed elsewhere are left to review.
      Later split in two: `no-env-in-service`, and `no-wall-clock`, which
      also covers every module of `app/shared`, `app/host`, `app/main`,
      `packages/engine` and `packages/contracts`. Its 29 sites there:
      5 allowed in place with their reasons, and 24 in 11 files the
      time-and-liveness hardening is replacing, off in `.oxlintrc.json`
      until each of its pull requests takes its file off the list
      (`lint/README.md`). Moving them to `Clock` alone would not have
      prevented this week's liveness bugs: 7 of the 8 wall-clock
      elapsed-time decisions in `app/shared` already read
      `Clock.currentTimeMillis`, which is `Date.now()`, so `EFFECT.md`
      now says elapsed time is monotonic.
- [x] Effect's own guidance for agents: `EFFECT.md` sends every agent to
      the installed package's `AGENTS.md` (byte-identical to `LLMS.md`
      at the pinned tag) and its `ai-docs/`, and to `SCHEMA.md` at the
      pinned tag, instead of a vendored copy, and adopts Effect's rules
      where it had no reason to differ. It keeps its own imports,
      module-level layers and tests on the real graph, each with a
      one-line reason. The new rules hold the lines a PR changes;
      known older code they don't sweep: 45 `JSON.parse` and 12
      hand-written object guards outside tests and the lab, 19
      single-tag `catchTags`, 20 `yield* Effect.fail(`.
- [x] `electron-log` for a real log file in `~/Library/Logs`.
- [ ] Bug fixes that belong on `main` now, not on the branch: the
      socket server re-adding a mid-handshake connection after stop,
      the palette mirror badge with device icons off (unverified), and
      the second audit round's bugs (handed to the bug thread).

### Step 1: contracts

- [x] zod to Schema across `shared/schemas`, keeping the JSON on the
      wire byte-identical. The golden fixtures in
      `packages/contracts/fixtures` hold it
      (`packages/contracts/test/fixtures.test.ts`). The schemas, the
      contract modules, `errors.ts`, the hub protocol and the
      predicates they borrow now live in `packages/contracts`; the
      transport (`app/shared/ipc/socket`, `transport.ts`,
      `registerContract.ts`, `buildClient.ts`, `client.ts`) stays in
      the app until step 4.
- [x] `Schema.TaggedError` end to end. The renderer stops matching
      message prefixes (`packages/contracts/src/errors.ts`, the
      command refusal included). The contract modules' own refusals
      still match message text: the forward's connect failure and
      too-many-streams, the mirror stop's unconfirmed copy and stayed
      copy, and the cancelled move, which a peer's answer rewords.
- [x] The contract's per-call flags become annotations on RPC groups,
      one per module (33 modules). The renderer's API object and the
      `HostApi` type are generated from the contract, retiring the
      triple listing in `client.ts` and the 140-line source-parsing
      test. A `grant` annotation ties the consent text to the calls it
      covers (`packages/contracts/src/grants.ts`, the table the proof
      holds to the annotations).
- [x] Results are validated on the client for remote invokes and
      pushes (today only the server checks outputs, and only in dev).
      The client decodes over every transport but this machine's own
      (`local`), in `shared/ipc/buildClient.ts`.
- [x] Protocol version in the contract and a golden encoded fixture
      per RPC that must still decode, so two 3.x builds cannot drift.
      `PROTOCOL_VERSION` (`packages/contracts/src/protocol.ts`) and
      `fixtures/wire/v<version>.json`, held by `test/wire.test.ts`, which proves
      this build reads the samples. No wire carries the version yet:
      the step 4 transport's handshake does.
- [x] Hub protocol to Schema, now `packages/contracts/src/hubProtocol.ts`.
      The connectInfo ask and answer in `shared/hub/link.ts` are still
      zod.

The socket frames and what parses through them (the socket server,
the control socket's hello) stay on zod, since step 4 deletes them;
zod leaves the catalog when step 4 removes its last user. A union of
`strict(...)` structs cannot pick its member by tag, so a refused
message lists every member's failure.

`@effect/vitest` 4.0.x needs vitest 5. The catalog is on vitest 5 and
the hub pins 4.1 on its own (its vitest-pool-workers supports ^4.1
only). `@effect/vitest` joins the catalog with its first `it.effect`
proof.

### Step 2: the engine

The Go CLI becomes `engine/`: pure Node TypeScript, Effect services, no
Electron import, enforced by the package graph. The Go `sm` stays the
shipped binary until the last box.

- [x] Scaffold, the SQLite store (decision 5) and its migrator, the
      one-time import from the JSON files. `node:sqlite` waits for a
      lock by blocking its thread, so a process holds one store: two
      connections in one process deadlock. The mirror files
      (`file-sync/*.json`), the icon cache and the clone records stay
      files until their owners move.
- [x] Data model services: paths, registry and state, identity,
      layout, config keys and defaults from a single Schema (closes the
      "setting that cannot be turned off" class for good), names,
      launcher catalog, package scripts.
      In `packages/engine`: `Paths`, `Config`, `Usage`, `Launchers`,
      `Scripts`, `Layout`, the names, `Registry`, `Identity`, `Icons`
      and `Terrier`. A `project.json` holding no keys reads as
      unconfigured. The lifecycle scripts (setup, teardown, port-pool)
      run in the worktrees thread's `Lifecycle`.
- [x] One `Git` service, merging `gitx.go` and `host/lib/git`
      (`packages/engine/src/Git.ts`). The switch-over happened:
      `gitx.go` went with the Go CLI, and `host/lib/git` with item 15
      (step 7, Leftover mechanisms), so the engine's `Git` is the one
      git the engine, `sm` and the host run.
- [x] The parity harness: the Go `sm --json` and the engine run against
      the same sandboxes and are diffed verb by verb. The `cli-reads`
      and `identity` proofs become this harness.
      In `packages/engine/test/parity.test.ts`: `config`, `projects
      config`, `launchers`, `run` and `projects list` with every
      identity scenario, and every worktree verb `cli-reads` drives.
      The two proofs are deleted in the switch-over PR.
- [x] The Go helper for the five darwin syscalls (decision 4), built
      and bundled like `file-sync`: `macfs/`, behind the engine's
      `Darwin` service. Who passes it the helper's path (Resources in
      the app, beside the binary for the terminal `sm`) is settled when
      the host and the CLI first build the engine's layer.
- [x] Worktrees: list, status, create, rm, relocate, reorder, marks.
      Clone checkout, carry-over and verification, with the
      differential fuzz test ported. Hygiene and disk usage as async
      walkers.
      In `packages/engine`: `Worktrees` (identities, rows and the shelf
      settlement, path, status, marks, describe, create, rm, move, rekey,
      adopt, setup, destination, `projects relocate`), `WorktreeData`,
      `GitHub`, `Lifecycle` (the setup, teardown and port-pool scripts),
      `CarryOver`, `CloneCheckout` with its verifier on `macfs lstat`
      (the fuzz test is `test/CloneCheckout.fuzz.test.ts`) and
      `Hygiene`. Each verb, `disk-usage` and `projects reorder`
      included, matches the Go `sm --json` in the parity harness. The
      tidy page's facts have no Go verb and are held to the host's rules
      directly. `sm rm --stack` comes with landing.
- [x] Land, merge, stack, PR, done, doctor, transfer, shelf, shell
      integration, `sm run` as a signal-forwarding child, and the
      updater, which gains the tests it never had.
      In `packages/engine`: `Landing` (pr, merge with auto-merge and
      merge queues, land, stacks through GitHub's stack merge or layer by
      layer, `rm --stack`, done) over `GitHub`'s pull request queries,
      each against Go with a scripted gh whose calls are compared too.
      `errorDocument` is Go's error message and code by tag, for the
      terminal and for land's document after a failed cleanup.
      `Updater` (check, stage, update, finish-install) works on the same
      files in the data dir as Go and the app, with Go's semver and JSON
      reading beside it (`semver`, `updateFeed`), and its deterministic
      modes run against the Go `sm update --json` in the parity harness.
      The terminal passes its version, arch, binary and pid with each call.
      `Doctor` runs Go's checks in Go's order and repairs with Go's
      prompts, its `--json` held to Go's on stale locks, gone repos,
      moved worktrees, prunable metadata and leftovers. It reads the
      store where Go reads files, and builds the store itself inside
      the run (`doctorLayer`): when the import of the JSON files is
      refused, it reads them leniently as Go does and reports the file
      in Go's words, and when the store won't open it says so beside the
      checks that need no state. Dormant state is store rows, so
      deleting them is a prompted repair where Go names a folder.
      `Transfer` (send, bring, mirror, unmirror, mirrors, devices, a
      peer's worktrees) over `Control`, which speaks Go's control wire

      against a scripted app.
      Shell integration is the engine's `shellHook` module (the wrapper

- [x] Schema-derived arbitraries with fast-check for the parsers and

      Effect 4's own `Arbitrary` stands in for fast-check, which it no

      readers of git's worktree list, status and refs, the gitignore
      escape, and the layout math (`cleanPath`, `isSameOrInside`, and
      every new worktree landing under a managed base), over
      `test/lib/properties.ts`.
- [x] The terminal entrypoint on `effect/cli`, built with
      `bun build --compile`. Menu and picker on ink. Commands return
      typed errors; the exit code derives from the error. Startup and
      size measured against the Go binary.
      Started in `packages/cli` with the `config` verbs, tested as the
      built binary beside the Go `sm`. Then `projects list`, `icon` and
      `config` (its shared verbs), and `launchers`, naming projects

      `doctor` over `doctorLayer`, which opens the store inside each

      worktree verbs over `Worktrees`,
      at the top level and under `worktrees`: `list`, `path`,
      `destination`, `status`, `describe`, `shelve`, `unshelve`,
      `autopull`, `agent-working`, `create`, `rm`, `move`, `adopt`,
      `setup`, `rekey` and `cd`. Then landing over `Landing`: `pr`,

      terminal tool through the chosen terminal), and `update` over
      `Updater` (`--check`, `--stage`, `--finish-install --pid`). Then
      the cross-device verbs over `Transfer`: `send`, `bring`, `mirror`,
      `unmirror`, `mirrors`, `devices` and `list --remote`/`--from`.
      Then the menus on ink, loaded only when one shows: the project,
      worktree and launcher pickers behind bare `cd`, `projects remove`,
      `open` and every worktree verb run without its target at a
      terminal, held to Go's keys and frame in a pty. Then the settings
      verbs left: `edit` (a copy of the settings in `$VISUAL` or

      `--default-branch` flags; and `--version`. The
      store opens through
      `@effect/sql-sqlite-bun` there: Bun's SQLite can't load
      extensions, which the node driver asks for, so `Store.layer`
      takes the process's driver. Startup and size are under
      measurements. Left for step 7, all cosmetic: ~~help and the
      parser's usage messages are effect/cli's words, not Go's (a usage
      error exits 2 like Go's but prints help to stdout first)~~ (Go's
      since #658); ~~a write
      to a project with no default branch names it in other words than
      Go~~ (Go's words since #656); ~~a stack land doesn't say when the
      primary checkout sits on a landed branch (Go's `sm done` hint)~~ (it
      does since #657); `list` and `status` name
      projects without the icon's color (decision 12).
- [x] Switch-over, one PR: the host imports the engine; `cliDelegate`,
      `cliRunner`, `lockFile.ts`, `jsonFile.ts` and the paths mirror
      go; `cli/` is deleted; the four "a CLI child exists" mechanisms
      (watcher echo suppression, the busy and quit prompt,
      `killAllCli`, `noteSelfWrite`) get explicit equivalents; the
      license generator and the dev loop drop the Go build for the CLI;
      `cliInstall` links the compiled binary.
      Left open: the updater's path is checked by the first
      `v3.0.0-alpha.N` prerelease on the real feed, with the real
      signing. It must show the app staging on the engine in-process,
      the bundled binary at `Contents/Resources/smv3` running `smv3
      update --finish-install` after the app quits, and the relaunch
      into the new bundle.
      With Go gone, nothing compares the frozen surface against it: the
      parity harness and the terminal's Go comparisons went, and their
      cases that hold on their own moved into the engine's and the
      terminal's tests. The compiled `sm` is about 73 MB.

### Step 3: the app core

Main and host to Effect, subsystem by subsystem, each behind its
existing Promise interface until its callers move.

- [x] Runtime and lifetimes: one layer graph, `Scope` finalizers in
      place of the quit chain in `main/index.ts`, the per-subsystem
      `stop()` latches and the 15-second `app.exit` backstop.
      `main/hostLayer.ts` and `main/shellLayer.ts` under one
      `ManagedRuntime` in `main/index.ts`. Each subsystem is still
      wrapped around its own start and stop, so the latches inside them
      (the tunnel runner, the hub connection, the direct plane, the
      mirror daemon, `markShuttingDown`) go as each one converts.
- [x] Processes through `effect/process`: cloudflared, the file-sync
      daemon, pty scripts behind a node-pty wrapper, git and gh. Move
      steps undo themselves with finalizers. The pid file for orphans
      stays.
      gh, terrier, `which`, the account probes and the login shell
      capture run through `host/lib/util/processes.ts`. The keychain
      delete stays `execFileSync`: it runs synchronously at module top,
      before the Clerk bridge, where nothing can await.
      The file-sync daemon and its serve children are children of the
      `FileSync` service's scope (`host/fileSync/FileSync.ts`), and the
      daemon restarts on `restartSchedule` (`shared/remote`).
      cloudflared is the `Tunnel` service's child
      (`host/direct/cloudflared.ts`): one supervision fiber per wanted
      port, the probe a Schedule, its proofs on `TestClock`.
      A script's PTY is a resource in a scope of its own under the
      `ScriptRuns` layer (`host/lib/scripts/pty.ts`), its release the
      kill chain. The CLI's lifecycle scripts, which are the CLI's
      children, keep their signal path until the switch-over.
      A pull, a send and a send's landing each run as one effect under
      the move's cancel, their steps' undo in finalizers
      (`host/lib/sync/landing.ts`, `move.ts`, `sourceLink.ts`). Their
      git is the engine's `Git`, which took over from
      `host/lib/git/core.ts` at the switch-over (step 7, Leftover
      mechanisms, item 15). Checked in the lab: a script's process group
      on cancel and at quit, a killed cloudflared's restart and a
      crashed run's orphan reaped, a killed daemon's restart, and a
      mirror session surviving a quit.
- [x] `Schedule` for reconnects and heartbeat, `Stream` for the git
      and state watchers, `Semaphore` and `Cache` for `limit`,
      `singleFlight` and `ttlCache`, `TestClock` for `fakeClock`.
      The hub link's supervisor, the direct keeper and the heartbeat

      `shared/remote/supervisor.ts`), seamed by the context they run
      in, and `fakeClock` is gone for `testClock()` over a TestClock.
      Still Promise-facing objects with `stop()`, since their owners
      are not Effect yet.
      The git watcher and the state watcher are services whose watches
      are `FileSystem.watch` streams, debounced, in their layers'
      scopes (`host/lib/GitWatcher.ts`, `main/electron/stateWatcher.ts`).
      gh's, terrier's and port-pool's answers are `Cache`s in the
      `GithubCli`, `Terrier` and `Ports` services
      (`host/lib/githubCli/GithubCli.ts`, `host/lib/terrier.ts`,
      `host/lib/Ports.ts`), and the villager download is the
      `VillagerData` service on a `Semaphore` and a `FiberHandle`.
      The other users of `limit`, `singleFlight` and `ttlCache` go with

      `git/repoIdentity`, `worktrees/carryOver` and `hygiene`

      the socket server and the ws client. Still on the helpers: the

      and `hygiene` (step 7, Leftover mechanisms).
- [x] `Logger` and `Tracer` for the console calls and `logFailure`,
      electron-log as the file sink, a local trace file per device so

      `effect/devtools` in dev.
      The logger is `app/shared/log.ts`, the tracer, `trace.log` and
      devtools (`SHIGOMORI_DEVTOOLS=1`) `app/main/observability.ts`.
      Promise code traces through `app/shared/trace.ts`. A move's spans
      carry `sourceWorktree` on every device it touches, which

- [x] `Config` for the env reads, `KeyValueStore` for electron-store.

      The Clerk tokens are `app/main/electron/ClerkTokens.ts`, a

- [x] While each file is open: split the five large orchestrators

      in place of three flags; one connection record in the socket
      server; `util.parseEnv` for the hand-written dotenv parser; one
      timeout-or-fallback helper in control; one peer-client seam.
      Done: `modules/sync.ts`, into its handlers, the landing

      and the receipts and teardown (`receipts.ts`). `mirror/gitFollow.ts`,
      into the follower, its decisions (`followPlan.ts`) and its
      transfers (`followTransfer.ts`).
      `modules/mirror.ts`, into its handlers, the sessions this device
      runs (`host/mirror/sessions.ts`), their decisions (`plan.ts`) and
      what it serves a peer (`serving.ts`). A session's kind is one
      `mode` label (`mirror`, `mirror-branch`, `transfer-<token>`) in
      place of `transfer`, `copySide` and `mirrorBranch`. Sessions from
      a v2 build keep their old labels, which `modeOf` reads until step
      7 deletes that fallback with the legacy sweep.
      The dev dotenv file and the engine's reading of port-pool's env
      files (`ports.ts`) use `util.parseEnv`.
      `modules/control.ts`, into its handlers, the ops
      (`host/lib/control/ops.ts`), their reads of the account and the
      peers (`peers.ts`) and their decisions (`plan.ts`). An ask that
      only informs an answer has one timeout-or-fallback,
      `host/lib/util/within.ts`, which the mirror's copy probe shares.
      Every peer client comes from one seam, `host/ipc/PeerReach.ts`,
      fed one transport, one channel source and this device's id.
      Left for step 4, which replaces them: `socket/server.ts` and its
      connection record, and merging the desktop and web account
      handlers (the web loopback goes with the utility-process host).

### Step 4: transport

The 3.0 wire break.

- [x] Effect RPC over Effect's socket for the device link, replacing
      `host/socket/server.ts`, `wsClientTransport.ts`, `channels.ts`,
      `frames.ts`, `heartbeat.ts` and `proof.ts` (about 1,800 lines
      plus the credit flow). Middleware carries the ticket handshake,
      the command gate and tracing. Request interruption retired the
      per-page `AbortController` rotation and most of the
      `HandlerContext.signal` plumbing (57 uses); the `moves.ts` cancel
      registry and the signal a move and a landing still take
      (`host/lib/util/abort.ts`) remain (step 7, Leftover mechanisms).
      Streaming RPCs replace broadcasts and listener sets.
      The link is one group (`app/shared/remote/link.ts`): every call
      annotated `remote`, every host push, and the link's own calls
      (`packages/contracts/src/modules/link.ts`: the handshake, a ping
      and the byte channels). The host serves it (`DeviceLink`,
      `app/host/socket/server.ts`) over a `ws` listener that keeps the
      Origin gate, the caps and the lockout, and the dialer
      (`app/shared/remote/deviceLink.ts`) is an `RpcClient` over Effect's
      socket. The handshake is two calls, `link:challenge` and
      `link:hello`, with the mutual ticket proof as before; the hello
      carries `PROTOCOL_VERSION` (now 4), and a host on another one
      refuses with `ProtocolVersionMismatchError`, which the dialer
      hands on as a blocked verdict. `PeerAuth` refuses every other
      call until the hello and provides the peer to handlers,
      `CommandGate` runs only `gated: false` calls without the switch
      (a missing annotation is gated), and spans continue across
      devices: a move's steps and the link's handlers run Promise code
      under their span (`host/lib/util/trace.ts`, moved from shared),
      so the `sourceWorktree` stand-in went. Pushes are streams the
      dialer subscribes to as a link opens, off `HostPushes`, and the
      `*Views` tables are served too. Every frame is Effect's binary
      layout (`RpcSerialization.layerSchemaBinary`, Effect 4's binary
      serialization; 4.0.2 ships no MsgPack), so byte channels carry
      raw bytes: numbered `read` and `write` calls of 32 KB at most, in
      place of the credit frames. The dialer keeps as many of each in
      flight as the link carries in a round trip plus about 20 ms of
      queue, learned from how long each call takes, up to 64 (2 MiB),
      so a transfer fills the link without the terminal or the calls
      beside it waiting behind its backlog (step 7 turned the read
      from a stream into calls, then sized the window).
      A schema the layout cannot compile fails its call, so the
      contracts' wire proof compiles every call and round-trips every
      sample through it (a union of two strings, `carryOverListing`'s
      `relative`, became one string). A lone UTF-16 surrogate crosses
      as U+FFFD, which no id, path or text a device reads holds. The
      hub relays no link traffic in step 4 (it carries `connectInfo`
      alone), so nothing there limits the layout, and a browser reads
      binary websocket frames as the desktop does. Errors cross as
      `CallFailureSchema`: the contract errors, or `RemoteCallError`
      with the far side's message and code. Liveness is the RPC
      client's pings plus a `link:ping` probe; the host still cuts a
      silent peer. Permessage-deflate replaces the deflated frames,
      offered on tunnel dials only.
      On the link, `HandlerContext.signal` is now each call's own,
      aborted by RPC interruption, beside a new `connection` signal for
      what follows a caller's connection (a script's event stream). The
      mirror start cancels the peer's send by interrupting the call.
      A send's landing is still cancelled by the link it
      runs under, not by interrupting `sync:receiveWorktree`, so a copy
      that lands as the cancel comes is still rolled back. zod stays
      in the catalog for the control wire's frames
      (`main/core/control/frames.ts`, which the CLI's move to this
      client retires) and the hub link (step 5), and the hub link
      keeps its own heartbeat (`shared/hub/heartbeat.ts`) until then.
      Checked in the lab on two dev devices: a send that clones the
      repo onto the peer with its uncommitted work, the peer killed
      and redialed, and a send cancelled while the peer's setup script
      hung (the script died and the copy went).
- [x] A clone from a peer for a project whose default branch is a
      remote-tracking ref failed with `Ref "refs/heads/origin/main" does
      not exist here.`: the owner's mirror of a worktree onto a device
      without the project. That is nearly every project, not one setting:
      the default's candidates go remote first (`pickDefaultRef`), so any
      clone with a fetched origin resolves to `origin/main`, and
      `projects add` seeds `defaultBranch` from that. Class: one fact
      used in two senses, the default branch as the ref a row is
      measured against and a worktree branches from, and as the local
      branch a clone checks out; and fixtures unlike a real project
      (here a remote added with nothing fetched from it), which never
      met the owner's settings (earlier: #923, a symlinked data folder;
      #930, a GitHub Enterprise host; the guide's wiring, #1012). The
      engine resolves the full ref (`Git.resolvePrimaryRef`) and names
      its local branch from it, so the branch equals the ref exactly
      when the ref is local; the host's owner, `defaultBranchOf`
      (`host/lib/projects`), answers both senses (`ref`, `branch`), and
      the clone's facts carry both: the source bundles its
      `refs/remotes/origin/main`, whose local `main` may lag or be
      missing, and the clone checks it out as `main`, with `origin/HEAD`
      and the upstream on `origin/main`. Remote-tracking refs join the
      bundle allowlist; a strict link answer gained a field, so protocol
      16, beta.2's one bump (the dev hub moves to floor 16 only after
      both of the owner's Macs run a build with 16). `pnpm test
      sync-transfer`'s "mirror start into a device with no checkout, the
      project's default branch a remote-tracking ref" fails on the line
      above without the fix, and `Worktrees.create.test`'s "names the
      default branch's local branch" without the engine's half.
      Siblings, every consumer of `primaryRef`, `primaryBranch`,
      `defaultBranch` and the clone facts in the host, engine, CLI and
      renderer: `primaryBranch` read a local default named like a remote
      ref (`origin/feat`) as `feat` and a removed remote's ref
      (`gone/trunk`) as itself, which a pull request's base, the
      landing's cleanup and hygiene compare with (fixed here, in the
      engine, for `primaryBranch`; `primaryTarget.remote` and hygiene's
      candidates still split the short name, so a local `origin/feat`
      reads as on remote `origin` there, and a remote-tracking ref with
      no slash (`refs/remotes/foo`, made by hand) reads as local:
      follow-ups that carry the full ref on the identity row); the clone's landing guard compared the landing branch
      with "origin/main" and now with `main` (fixed here);
      `projects:defaultBranch`, the new-worktree base, quick create and
      Manage branches take the ref, and the merged-branch box and
      checkout mode map it to its branch with `localBranchOf` (right,
      but for a local and a remote `origin/feat` both there, where
      `localBranchOf` reads the remote's and the engine the local: a
      follow-up to read the row's `primaryBranch`); a sync and a merge from primary take the ref (right); a
      pull request's draft diffs against the ref (right);
      `SHIGOMORI_DEFAULT_BRANCH` is the ref in both the engine's and the
      host's script env (right, and the same); a send's or a bring's
      landing branches from the incoming ref and reads no default branch
      (none). Left as they are: the allowlist admits any
      `refs/remotes/*` to a peer holding the grant, which already
      reaches every branch; a remote ref lands at
      `refs/shigomori/incoming/remotes/...`, which a local branch named
      `remotes/...` would share, and only a clone, into a fresh repo,
      asks for one; with `upstream/main` as the default, the clone takes
      upstream's tip as its `origin/main` while its `origin` is the
      source's origin, as `git clone` of a fork reads after its first
      fetch. Left for the cross-device sweep: a clone takes none of the
      source's project settings (carry-over, setup script), only what
      `projects add` seeds.
- [x] The host as a utility process on loopback (decision 6). The
      Electron IPC bridge, the preload transport and the web loopback
      binding are deleted rather than converted.
      First, the window is on RPC: the shell's modules (`ShellGroup`,
      every client module but `hub` and `portForward`) over a
      MessagePort the preload hands the page (`app/shared/ipc/shell.ts`,
      `app/main/ipc/shellLink.ts`), and the host's (`LoopbackGroup`,
      `isHostSide`) over the loopback, dialed with the token the shell
      answers on `window:hostAddress` (`app/renderer/hostLink.ts`). A
      link that drops is dialed again at the address the shell answers
      then. The IPC bridge, the preload transport and the per-page
      `AbortController` rotation went: a page that goes takes its links
      with it, which is the `connection` signal. `sync:cancelMove`
      stays, since the CLI and a peer's mirror cancel by key. The dev
      renderer serves effect unbundled and deduped, since the contracts
      reach it through their own link, and the binary codecs of one
      copy cannot read schemas another built.
      Then the host's composition root moved out of `main/`: its graph,
      handlers and wires are `app/host/process/` (`layer.ts`,
      `handlers.ts`, `wires.ts`), with `main/core`'s git watcher, mirror
      and port-forward engines beside them, and none of it reaches
      Electron. Now the host is an Electron utility process
      (`host/process/host.ts`), which `main/hostProcess.ts` forks with
      its facts on the command line (version, paths, the window's
      origin). The shell talks to it over the loopback like any
      device, through the session (`modules/session.ts`: the account's
      facts at start and on every change, window focus, wake, the busy
      count, the updater's state, quit). The host calls back over the
      port the shell hands its process (`modules/shellCalls.ts`:
      ready with its address and device id, a failed start with the
      doctor's report, relaunch, the updater). The device id is the
      host's, from its store: the window gets it with the host's
      address, and the shell when the host says it is up. The updater
      stays in the shell (decision 6) on a small engine graph of its
      own, and the shell runs its own child-process spawner for the
      account's probes. Quit asks the host what is running, then asks
      it to stop and waits for it to exit before closing the shell's
      graph. A host that exits unasked is forked again (three times a
      minute at most), told the account again, and redialed by every
      window. The host's console goes to the shell's log with a
      `[host]` prefix, and its spans to `host-trace.log`. The Promise
      adapters left in the host's graph serve the host's own code that
      is not Effect yet. The shell reaches none of them. Checked in the
      lab on a dev device: launch, quit, a second instance, the host
      killed and redialed, and a relaunch. Last, the web client: it is a
      device with no host, reaching every host as a peer over the
      device link through the hub, and the web loopback binding went.
      What the tab serves itself (the client modules and its copy of
      the shared settings) is one local registrar
      (`app/web/ipc/localRegistrar.ts`). A host call nothing there
      serves still gets the fail-closed answers, until step 6 stops the
      renderer asking a host the browser does not have. Its tabs each
      link to a host (designs, "The web client's tabs";
      `app/test/web-tabs.mts`). Checked in the lab against the dev hub:
      two tabs of one profile each linked to a dev device, read from it
      and heard its push, and a reload left the other linked. The older
      tab's hub socket reads superseded until step 5. The Promise
      adapters left in the host's graph go in step 7.
- [x] The CLI control wire uses the same RPC client from the engine's
      terminal entrypoint, so three wire loops become one.
      The host serves the device link a second time on loopback
      (`app/host/socket/loopback.ts`, the `Loopback` service): every
      host call with its pushes and views, and the control contract
      (`LoopbackGroup`). Its handshake is the link's, with a token minted
      as the host starts standing in for the ticket, published with the
      port in an owner-only `loopback.json` and handed out by the
      service's `address`. The engine's `Control` is an RpcClient over
      it, in Effect's binary layout. `control:send` and `control:bring`
      stream their progress, then their answer, since a caller-only push
      raced the answer on separate streams. The group, its middleware,
      the handshake proof and the module list moved to contracts
      (`link.ts`, `proof.ts`, `allModules.ts`), so the terminal builds
      the same group. `control.json`, `main/core/control/` and its zod
      went. zod stays in the catalog for the hub link's connectInfo ask
      (`app/shared/hub/link.ts`).
- [x] Streams in place of invalidation (decision 7) on the host side:
      each view a stream from the store's change notifications.
      The source is the engine's `StoreChanges`: a version per table,
      moved by triggers, read when `total_changes()` or `data_version`
      moves, looked at on each write to the store's write-ahead log, so
      the app's own writes count as well as the terminal's. A view (`app/host/lib/views.ts`) re-reads its
      query on the store's changes and the host's pushes (`HostPushes`),
      and emits only what differs. Each host module's `*Views` table
      serves its contract's views: `projects:watch`, `worktrees:watch`,
      `worktrees:watchChangeStatus`, `ports:watch`, `scripts:watch`
      (the Live page's runs), `mirror:watch` and the settings,
      `globalConfig:watch` and `sharedSettings:watch`. Each value is
      what the query beside it answers, and `app/test/host-views.mts`
      holds them. The viewer cache ping, `movesHostState` and the git
      watcher's self-echo suppression went: a store write from any
      process pushes `git:externalChange`, and the app's own git writes
      ping `git:projectChanged` like anyone's, which is the adapter that
      keeps the renderer on today's pushes until step 6.
      Left open: the `*Views` tables are served on the device link,
      and on the loopback once it exists. A worktree's changes and
      its ports also poll (2 s, 5 s), since a file edit or a server
      coming up tells the host nothing.

### Step 5: the hub

- [x] Routes on `effect/http-api` with a typed client, the shared
      Schema protocol, Durable Object storage through Effect's sqlite
      driver, the Worker calling its object directly instead of through
      internal fetch routes, the unreachable revoked-table fallback
      removed.
      `HubApi` (`packages/contracts/src/hubApi.ts`) is served by
      `hub/src/worker.ts` and called through `HttpApiClient` in
      `app/shared/account/service.ts`, which keeps its Promise face. A
      refusal crosses as its tagged error (`HubDeviceRevokedError`,
      `HubAccountFullError`, ...) in place of `{ error, code }`, and an
      unnamed status (429, a bare 5xx) as an `HttpClientError`. The
      object's tickets are a table in its SQLite storage through
      `@effect/sql-sqlite-do`. The object's methods (`mintTicket`,
      `online`, `revoke`) are called directly. Its fetch serves only the
      websocket upgrade, which a method call cannot carry. The
      connectInfo ask and answer are Schema in `hubProtocol.ts`, and zod
      left the catalog. D1 stays behind the `Registry` service on its
      own binding: `@effect/sql-d1` 4.0.2 needs a `workers-types`
      release still inside the cooldown. The Cloudflare tunnel client
      (`hub/src/tunnel.ts`) stays Promise code, called from Effect.
- [x] The version floor enforced: a v2 device is turned away with a
      clear message. Dev hub during the branch, production at cutover.
      Every app call names its `PROTOCOL_VERSION` in an `sm-protocol`
      header, and the hub's `ProtocolFloor` middleware turns away a
      build naming none or one below `HUB_PROTOCOL_FLOOR` (4) with
      `HubUpdateRequiredError`, before any credential is read. It is a
      403 whose body carries the sentence in an `error` field, which a
      v2 build reads as a refusal: its hub socket blocks with "ticket
      mint failed: ... Update it to connect." and stops retrying. A v3
      build blocks as `update-required` and the account page says so
      instead of offering to sign in again (the fake host poses it with
      `?hubBlocked=`). The connect route needs no check: its ticket
      comes from a mint, which the floor guards. Production gets the
      floor with the cutover deploy.
- [x] The web client's tabs (designs): the Durable Object keys a web
      device's connections by device and connection id, relays to each,
      and supersedes only a desktop device's duplicate.
      The hub connection mints its connection id once and sends it with
      every ticket mint, so a redial replaces its own stale socket. The
      hub takes the device's kind from its row (platform `web`), and
      the ticket carries both into the object, which tags each socket
      by device and by connection. A desktop's new socket supersedes all
      of the device's, and a web tab's only its own connection's. Relay
      reaches every socket of a device and presence names it once. The
      hub link's ask ids start at random, since an answer reaches every
      tab. Held by `hub/test/hub.spec.ts` ("a web device's tabs") and
      `app/test/web-tabs.mts` over the stub hub, which keys tabs the same
      way. Checked in the lab against the dev hub: two Helium tabs of one
      profile, both hub sockets connected and linked to a dev device, a
      reload of one leaving the other connected, the desktop listing the
      web device once. The same check found the hub client sending
      `traceparent` and `b3`, which the hub's CORS refuses, so every
      browser call failed its preflight since step 5's first box. The
      client sends no trace headers now. Left open: signing out in one
      tab while another tab of the profile holds its Clerk session
      enrolls the profile again from that tab.

### Step 6: the renderer

- [x] Views and containers (decision 8), first, before atom-react.
      The closed PR #461's head commit is the reference for the split
      (56 views, 8 scenes, a `scenes` proof that renders every view in
      Node). Port it rather than cherry-pick it: `main` has moved
      under it. Landed as 416 views in 246 files, 200 containers in 185
      files, and 48 scenes over the fixtures in `app/lab/fake-host`. The
      `scenes` proof renders every scene in Node, checks that some scene
      draws every view, and checks that no container has markup of its
      own. A router link reaches a view as a `render…` prop, and a
      child container as a slot.
- [x] The `ui` package: views, primitives, the stylesheets, the fixture
      world. Theme classes scoped to the app's root element instead of
      `<html>`, the phone layout on container queries, no globals.
      - [x] The package, with the primitives. `packages/ui` holds
            `components/ui`, the hooks and helpers they read and the
            launcher icons; `check:ui` runs its boundary proof. A view
            opens links through `outside.tsx`, which the app fills.
      - [x] The stylesheets and fonts, in `src/styles`. The app's
            `renderer/app.css` imports them and names its own sources.
      - [x] The views, all 416 of them, by area, with the models,
            helpers and hooks only they read. The types the app's hooks
            and store build for them are defined in `ui`; helpers the
            host reads too went to `contracts`.
      - [x] The fixture world: the fixtures and scenes in `src/fixtures`
            and `src/scenes`, with the sidebar, inbox, grid and palette
            row builders they share with the app, and the `scenes` proof
            running in the package (the containers check stays in the
            app). Worktree fixture ids are twelve hex digits, so the web
            fake host loads the changes page again.
      - [x] The theme on the window's root. The theme classes,
            `data-palette` and `data-shell` live on the app's `#root`
            (a scene's on its own), menus, popovers and dialogs mount
            inside it (`root.tsx`), and no rule selects `:root`, `html`
            or `body`. The phone layout is a container query on the web
            shell's root under 48rem, so a phone-width frame takes it,
            and `usePhoneLayout` measures the root. A view reads no
            global and lays out by the window's width (`@min-*`), not
            the viewport's. The boundary proof holds both. The scenes
            compare to `release/v3` within anti-aliasing (4 of 96 shots
            differ, by at most 4 pixels).
- [x] The on-demand scene comparison against `main`, by hand or in CI,
      never on commit. `pnpm scenes:compare --base <ref>`
      (`app/lab/scene-compare`) shoots every scene in light and dark,
      each at its own window, for the working tree and the base, and
      reports the pairs that differ by more than 20 pixels, with
      images. The base is `origin/release/v3` until the branch is in
      main, then `origin/main`. The terminal's canvas is masked, since
      headless WebGL draws blank. The Scenes workflow runs it on
      demand and uploads the report.
- [x] Data layer onto the RPC client on one managed runtime, React
      Query untouched. Then the go/no-go: `@effect/atom-react` with
      reactivity keys in place of manual invalidation.
      The runtime is in: each client (a desktop window, a web client
      tab, the fake host) builds one `ManagedRuntime` from its links
      (`renderer/lib/runtime`) as the page boots and disposes it as
      the page goes, and `window.api`'s calls, views and pushes run as
      fibers on it. A connection has an Effect face (`Link`:
      `call`, `view`, `pushes`, in `shared/ipc/transport.ts`) and one
      Promise face over it (`linkTransport`); the device link's dial is
      a scoped `dialDevice`, the loopback redials on a `Schedule`, and a
      peer is a link over the hub hop (`peerLink`, `window.api.peerApi`).
      `electronApi.ts`, `hubTransport.ts` and `subscriberRegistry.ts`
      went. The boot's push wiring (`hostWatch.ts`), the terminal feed's
      retry ladder and the `useSyncExternalStore` stores stay as they
      are: their subscriptions already run on the runtime through
      `window.api`, and what is left in them is the invalidation and the
      stores' state, which the go/no-go decides. Checked in the lab on
      two dev devices and the web client over the dev hub: a peer's
      pages, its pushes and a terminal attached through `hub:watchPeer`,
      from a window and from the browser. That found a void answer
      failing the hub hop's binary layout on the desktop (#722).
      The go/no-go was a go for views only (decision 8): what a host
      serves as a view is an atom (`renderer/lib/runtime/atoms.ts`),
      and a request stays a React Query query, with no reactivity keys
      fed by pushes. The projects, worktrees, mirrors, running scripts,
      ports, terminals, the config, sharing and the shared settings
      went over (#726, #728 and this one), and their queries, cache
      writers and push handlers went with them. A view redials a
      dropped link, and a peer's refusal with a backoff to 30 s, for as
      long as anything reads it. What stays: the git, GitHub CLI and
      updater requests that `hostWatch.ts` invalidates, and the stores
      that keep a stream or a feed rather than a view (`scriptRuns`,
      `worktreeLifecycle`, the terminal drawer, the remote devices, the
      hub status, git fetch activity).
- [x] UI library adoptions: Base UI `Dialog` behind `ModalShell` (focus
      trapping and return for the 13 dialogs; as `dom.ts` admits,
      none of them traps focus today), Tabs and RadioGroup for
      `useRovingPick` and the segmented control, the router's match
      hook for `routePaths`, `electron-context-menu` for
      `contextMenu.ts`.
      Every `ModalShell` is Base UI's dialog: focus stays in it and
      returns to its opener, the page under it is hidden from assistive
      tech, and it takes a `label`. Its Escape stack and its exit copy
      stay ours, since callers close a shell by unmounting it. There
      were 14, the error details' included. The device tabs and the
      terminal tabs are `Tabs`, the segmented control and the sidebar's
      device filter `RadioGroup` (the control read as toggle buttons
      before), and `useRovingPick` kept only its scroll into view
      (`useRevealPicked`). The sidebar's matches are `useMatch` and
      `useParams`; `fillRoutePath` stays for the routes handed to the
      shell as strings. The right-click menu is
      `electron-context-menu` 5.1.0, laid out with every item the old
      one had (Open Link, Undo and Redo, Paste and Match Style, Select
      All where they showed), and the platform's Learn Spelling, Copy
      Image and macOS Writing Tools and AutoFill besides.
- [x] One settings form for every device, one transfer-actions
      component for both directions, one tone table, the class strings
      and plurals the audit listed.
      This machine and a peer get the same host sections
      (`deviceSections`), differing by data: commands granted, the
      read-only note, the save error, and the danger zone only this
      machine has. `TransferActionsView` draws Mirror and Transplant
      both ways. `status-dot.tsx` is the one tone table: the status
      word's step (`TONE_TEXT`), the mark's (`TONE_MARK`, which the
      pull request's, the sidebar pills' and the sync button's tables
      were), the wash, and the pill, with violet and indigo. Each view
      kept its step, so no shade moved. The class strings went into
      `IconButton` (`reveal`, `size="xs"`), `ToneTag`, `RowTag`,
      `FooterRow` and `EmptyPanel`, and 11 hand-rolled plurals into
      `pluralize`. Left: the copies whose verb agrees with the count
      ("is", "are") and the raw tones in banners and villager art,
      which no table holds.
- [x] The marketing site on the `ui` package: build-time views, live
      frames over the fixture-backed host layer, `og.jpg` shot from the
      hero scene in CI.
      Every picture on the site is a frame of the app's views over the
      fixtures, rendered at build time and scaled in CSS
      (`marketing/README.md`). The hero and the two Looks windows go
      live after the page loads, as they near the screen: the app's
      windows mounted in-process over the fake host's bridge
      (`app/lab/fake-host/frames.tsx`), one client for all of them. That
      needed a window to mount into any element (#737), the package's
      stylesheet and views kept inside the root, its wallpapers as files
      and no project icons in the fixtures so the CSP stays as it was
      (#739), and the open project followed per window (#741). The page
      themes the frames' roots. The hero scene is posed like the
      fixture world, so the swap keeps the picture but for the
      description's list, which a server draws as text. The phone,
      Devices (under Settings in the app) and the parts stay drawn at
      build time. The Link preview workflow shoots `og.jpg` from the
      built hero on `main` and opens a pull request when it changes;
      opening one needs the repository to let Actions create pull
      requests. The anatomy legend's "The branch" reads "What it's
      called", what the row shows now, until the owner words it.

### Step 7: sweep and ship

- [x] Delete every pre-v3 migration (legacy mirror cleanup,
      `adoptLegacyGrant`, the default-name migration,
      `dropRemovedLanKeys`, `state.json` to `registry.json`, and
      `modeOf`'s fallback to a session's pre-`mode` labels in
      `host/mirror/registry.ts`).
      The import reads what the last v2 release wrote, and the mirror
      daemon rewrites a v2 session's labels into `mode` on load
      (upgrade code, like the `wt/` move). The pre-2.0 data dir and
      its `root` pointer stay: a location, not a shape.
- [x] `pnpm licenses list` in place of `license-checker`.
      `pnpm list --prod` replaced it, since under the hoisted linker
      `licenses list` reads every package as Unknown and leaves out
      installed optional peers.
- [x] Shared fakes and sandboxes for the direct-plane, sync and mirror
      proofs; the fuller golden file in place of `socket-host`'s
      spot-checks.
      The proofs share one direct sandbox, its two devices named by
      role (`DIALER`, `LISTENER`), the mirror's fakes and the repo
      fixtures (`app/test/README.md`, "Shared fakes"). Every call's
      classification is generated into
      `packages/contracts/fixtures/wire/classification.json`, which
      replaces the spot-checks and `read-surface.golden.json`
      (`packages/contracts/README.md`, "Classification"). With them,
      the terminal's flaky "filters by name, and esc cancels" (timed
      out on CI twice): a key typed 150 ms after a frame could reach a
      menu not yet taking input, so the menu now draws its help line
      once its keys reach it, and the test types on that line. Times
      in Measurements.
- [x] The proofs that flaked during step 1: `sync-transfer`'s
      cancelMove, `git-watcher`, and the hub's 429 test.
- [x] `shell-files`' "the updater's bridge: stops looking with its
      scope" failed once on CI (run 38856509179, on #922, which doesn't
      touch the bridge) and passed on its rerun and 3 of 3 locally: after
      the 2.5 s wait the request file was gone while nothing had been
      asked, so something claimed or dropped it. Suspected: the fixed
      2.5 s sleep on a loaded runner, with a bridge from an earlier case
      still looking when the request lands. A second failure gets its
      timing fixed rather than a rerun.
      It failed twice more (#932, and run 38965923760 on #939). The
      cause was not the sleep's length: the bridge looks once as it
      starts, and that look's rename could still be waiting on libuv's
      thread pool after the scope closed. The fiber stopped, but the
      rename ran later and claimed the request the proof wrote after
      the stop. The proof now runs the bridge on a `TestClock` and
      counts its looks through its `FileSystem`. It stops the bridge
      only once the looks it saw have settled, moves the clock a minute,
      and expects no new look. With the scope's stop removed (a
      detached fiber), it fails. 25 of 25 passed with every core busy.
- [x] The `host-restart` walk's "the whole app stopped past every
      limit … a commit sent as it stopped lands once" failed once, in
      the `Walks` run after #957 (run 39005903253, 2026-10-17): the
      commit hadn't landed 30 s after the app resumed (the subject read
      "Touch several files"). #957 changed only the tunnel runner,
      which the signed-out lab build doesn't run, and the runs after
      #953 and #956 passed this case; the failed job passed when run
      again. Not closed by that rerun: if it fails again, on any
      landing, it is an item with a cause, either the walk's oracle
      (the 30 s poll against a 130 s whole-app stop) or the product
      after #953's change to the link's probe, and no further rerun.
      It failed again on the landings of 2026-10-17 (runs 39030636341,
      39058942434), and "a window whose timers ran late past every limit"
      beside it (39023765355, 39030636341, 39040618197, 39056310510,
      39058942434, 39061305388). The walks now keep the app's logs, the
      shell's and the host's trace, and upload them when one fails, which
      gave each its cause on runs made for it. The window's case was the
      walk's: the case before it stops the host until the watchdog starts
      another, whose band says so for 10 s on a timer in the renderer, and
      this case froze that renderer inside those 10 s, so on its resume a
      re-render could come before the band went and the record read it as
      this case's (39062984025). That case now ends once its band has
      gone. The commit's was the product's (39065117569, 39067315706): the
      commit reached the host before the stop and ran after it, beside the
      reads every view asked again as the app came back, and a `git
      status` and a `git diff` among them wrote back the index they
      refreshed, holding `index.lock`, so the commit's `git reset` failed
      on the lock. The stop only lined them up; a commit beside any view's
      read, or an editor's `git status`, could fail the same way. A write
      that takes the lock before it changes anything (add, apply,
      checkout, commit, mv, read-tree, reset, restore, rm, switch,
      update-index) and is refused because another git holds it is asked
      again, from 50 ms doubling, five times (`Git.test`'s "a write waits
      out the moment another git holds index.lock"); a refused one
      changed nothing, so nothing runs twice. Turning the app's optional
      locks off (`GIT_OPTIONAL_LOCKS=0`, so its `git status` writes
      nothing back) was tried and taken back: the index then stayed
      stale for the next outside `git status` to write back (the
      `stashes` walk's own poll), and a stash, recorded and then refused
      the lock for the reset that cleans the worktree, left it dirty (run
      39069475259); asked again, it would record a second stash. The
      mirror's apply of a peer's index runs under the worktree's index
      lock, and its own hand-counted retry of `read-tree` is gone. Left:
      a pull, a merge, a rebase and a stash, which can be refused after
      their first step, aren't asked again and fail on a held lock as
      git's own do.
- [x] The `stashes` walk's "stashes the changes under a name" failed
      once, in the `Walks` run after #1005 (run 39121296109,
      2026-10-18): the worktree still read dirty when the poll timed out
      (`D  NOTES.md`, ` M media/logo.png`, …). #1005 changed only the
      sidebar's, the palette's and the home grid's row builders, which no
      stash touches, and the runs after #1006 to #1012 passed it: a
      flake. Its symptom is the one above (a stash recorded, then its
      reset refused the index lock), so a second failure is that item
      come back, not a rerun.
- [x] The `new-worktree` walk's "branches off a source under the name
      typed" failed in the `Walks` run after #1014 (run 39164334300,
      2026-10-18): the branch came out `sylviawalk-branch`. The picked
      animal name (doubutsuNames, the owner's setting) lands after the
      page opens with the Branch name field focused, and the walk's fill
      had taken the empty field's selection before it and typed after,
      so the name went in after the seed; a person typing as the page
      opens met the same. Class: an async default written into a field
      the person may already own. The field keeps the seed only until
      it is typed in (as before), and a value set from outside under
      focus is now selected, so what is typed next replaces it (`Input`'s
      `selectOutsideChanges`, on the branch name); `pnpm test
      seeded-field` mounts the form and fails on `sylviawalk-branch`
      without it, and the walk's heading check is exact, where a
      substring passed the joined name. Siblings, every field a value can
      reach after it shows: the pull request dialog's title, autofocused
      while the draft's title loads, took the same opt-in (fixed here);
      the inline name editor (a device's or a worktree folder's rename)
      reset its draft to every stored name that arrived, throwing away a
      rename being typed, and now follows the name only while untouched,
      with the opt-in (fixed here, the same proof); the merge dialog's
      squash message renders once the preview it is seeded from is in,
      and a refetch of that preview while it is shown and untouched still
      replaces it, which is what an untouched seed is for; the worktree
      location's custom folder and the mirror's, transplant's and add

      description and branch editors copy the value as editing starts;
      the folder field is seeded in the click that shows it; the
      auto-shelve days and a forward's port show the live value until
      edited, and a change from another device in the instant a person
      starts typing there would join it (left: the same opt-in when it
      shows up). The seed in a focused field now arrives selected, so
      extending it takes an End first.
- [x] The flows between devices are walked after every landing
      (`pnpm walks cross-device`, `app/lab/walks.md`, "Two devices, a
      real project"), where before each had unit proofs against a
      simulated peer and a hand check before a cut, and none ran after
      a landing. Class: the user's configuration never ran (#1014's
      clone, #923, #930, #1012). The lab build runs as several devices
      of one account: a test flavor takes a profile (its own userData,
      lock and credential; `pnpm test build-profiles` holds prod and v3
      to ignoring one), and they enroll with this checkout's hub under
      `wrangler dev` (`hub/src/lab.ts`, a `lab:<account>` login in place
      of Clerk), so it runs the same in CI with no network. The project
      is this repository at a pinned commit, served by `git daemon` as
      its remote, with the owner's settings: default branch
      `origin/main`, carry-over of `CLAUDE.local.md` (ignored only by
      the user's excludes) and `app/.env.local`, a setup script,
      squash, doubutsuNames. Walked, each from the window it starts in:
      the other device's worktree read-only with its control off and its
      actions once on; mirror to and from, send and bring, into the
      project at another path and into a device without it. On a build
      without #1014 all eight transfers fail with the window's `Ref
      "refs/heads/origin/main" does not exist here.`; with it they pass.
      The `Walks` workflow checks out the whole history for the pin.
      The lab's hub settings reach a packaged build racily, a lab-only
      matter: the account service reads `SM_DEVICE_HUB_URL` and its key
      once, cached, and whether that first read comes before or after
      the login-shell rebuild (which keeps only `SHIGOMORI_DATA_DIR` of
      the launch environment) is a race, so the walks put them in the
      launch environment and the shell's startup files both. Class: a
      value read once at startup, before or after the login-shell
      rebuild. No setting a user could hit is read that way: the update
      endpoints' overrides are taken before the rebuild starts, and
      `SHIGOMORI_DIAL_KINDS` and `SHIGOMORI_DIRECT_FRONT_PORT` never reach
      a packaged build's host, every time (dev-only knobs).
- [x] `Store.crash.test`'s "an import killed anywhere in its run" failed
      twice, each "most runs were cut short (17 of 40)" and green on a
      rerun: on CI (run 38998107984, job 117295032411, on #953, which
      doesn't touch the store), and locally on #964's branch under
      lefthook's load, which has no run id. The cause was the one #940
      fixed for the updater's bridge, a proof sized by timing: it killed
      at forty fractions of a first run's measured length, so a slow
      first run put the kills past the end of the faster runs after it.
      The open now counts its events, each SQLite statement and file
      removal (`test/fixtures/killAt.ts`, imported before the platform
      binds `fs.rm`). The whole run counts them, and each of the forty
      runs SIGKILLs itself just before one of them, spread from the
      first to the last, so every one is cut, under any load.
- [x] `git-watcher`'s "real repository" failed again on CI after its
      step 1 fix (run 38910232031, on #927, which doesn't touch the
      watcher): "timed out waiting for the new worktree to ping", the
      `git worktree add` after the nested worktree's removal raising
      no project change within the wait.
      Not a fixed sleep: the step that failed is a 5 s wait for a ping
      that never came, and the run kept a passing run's pace up to it.
      0 of 15 failed locally with every core busy. The likely cause is
      the drop recorded with the updater's bridge (#919): FSEvents drops
      a batch under load, and libuv skips its dropped-event flags, so
      the watcher never sees the `worktrees/later` writes. That is the
      watcher's own gap and not the proof's. It is left as it is: only
      a periodic reconcile would close it, and a missed ping is caught
      up by the next change to the repository. (The app's watcher has
      reconciled on a beat since, the retrospective's O04, below; this
      proof still pins the events alone.)
- [x] Two proofs failed under load in the pre-commit hook's full run on
      a busy Lychee (2026-10-18, #1014's commits), each once and a
      different one each run, neither touched by the change, and each
      passed alone and in CI: `sync-transfer` (1 of 25), and
      `git-watcher`'s "backstop: a commit whose events were all lost is
      still signalled, once, …" with "one signal per move, not per
      tick", the beat seeing the commit's move twice. A second failure
      of either in CI is an item, not a rerun.
- [x] A watch's event is a hint, and a reconcile the backstop (the
      retrospective of 2026-10-17, O04), as the store's changes have a
      ten-second tick (engine `StoreChanges`). Every 10 s the git
      watcher (`host/lib/GitWatcher.ts`) reconciles: it starts again any
      watch that ended, a git directory's or a working tree's, signals
      the project whose git state moved since it last looked
      (`gitStateOf`: the HEADs, packed-refs, every loose ref's inode and
      stamp, locks left out, and the stopped-operation markers, read with
      no git, and left alone for a beat while a watch's debounce is
      running), and says a working tree whose watch had ended changed,
      its edits meanwhile unseen, once until that watch hears something.
      A reading that fails is skipped, not read as a change. A
      project's files are read with no lock held, waited on for 5 s,
      and never read again while a read of them is still out, so one
      on a volume that stalls (#932's open) holds one thread at most,
      holds the other projects up once, for those 5 s, and is skipped
      on a doubling wait after (2 to 32 beats), its events signalled
      without a read meanwhile. The mirror's index watch
      (`mirror/gitState.ts`) reads the index's stamp on the same beat,
      one read at a time, and fires for one that moved with no event
      (`pnpm test git-watcher`: with the git directory's events lost on
      purpose, a commit and a checkout are each signalled once, a
      working tree's ended watch is watched again, one that fails every
      time is said changed once, a repository whose git directory went
      is signalled, a project whose reading hangs leaves
      another's lost commit signalled and is read again only on its
      wait, and a stage the index watch never heard fires once).
      Left: an edit inside a working tree whose live watch dropped its
      event is still seen only at the next event or focus; reading a
      tree's files on a beat is what `git status` costs.
- [x] The terminal's help and usage messages, effect/cli's words since
      step 2 (`sm agents` says one line per verb where Go said more):
      decide whether Go's wording comes back.
      It did: the help is the Go `sm`'s catalog and layout
      (`packages/cli/src/help.ts`), and a refused command line says
      Go's sentence and exits 2, with no help page.
      ~~Left open: the Go `sm`'s `app`, `worktrees switch`, `worktrees link`
      and `projects relocate` never came to the terminal, so the help
      doesn't list them.~~ All four are in (#659, #660, #661, #663).
- [x] The device link's byte channels: the read stream's acknowledgement,
      one round trip per batch, is the half second left between a 10 MB
      bundle over an 80 ms link (4.6 s) and the old wire (4.1 s). Tune
      the batch size, or a prefetch window of a few batches in flight.
      A send carrying a 10 MB uncommitted file drops its link after
      about 52 s, on plain `release/v3` too (found in the end-to-end
      encryption lab).
      `pnpm test bench/wire` fails at start: its fixture
      (`test/lib/directBoot.mts`) needs vitest's runner.
      The bench runs again: the proofs' host runtime is its own module
      (`test/lib/hostRuntime.mts`), which vitest's setup file starts.
      The drop was the RPC client: a stream's pieces go into a queue of
      16 inside the socket's read loop, acknowledged as queued, so a
      reader that holds its pieces (a send's source writing its bundle
      while the destination's progress lines wait) stopped every call on
      the link once 16 were waiting, the pings too, until the ping
      timeout cut it (15 s, then 40). The host's bytes are now numbered
      `read` calls like the writes, eight in flight, the next asked only
      once the endpoint took a piece, so nothing waits on the socket for
      a busy reader (protocol 9). A channel's calls leave the link's
      in-flight cap, and the host refuses one more than eight ahead.
      Tuned by the bench, which now runs a terminal stand-in and calls
      beside a bundle (its shaped link also stopped reordering bytes,
      which a timer per chunk could). Any fixed count either fills the
      80 ms link and queues a second of the transfer in front of the
      terminal, or keeps the queue short and leaves the link half
      empty, and the right count differs by link. So the dialer sizes
      each direction's window to the link: what a round trip carries,
      learned from the quickest call, plus 20 ms of queue, at least two
      calls, at most 64 calls of 32 KB (2 MiB), each end holding at most
      that per channel (protocol 10). Numbers in Measurements.
- [x] The host's Promise adapters: 16 host services keep a Promise face
      (`app/host/lib/util/promiseAdapter.ts`), and 26 host modules
      outside the composition root (`app/host/process/`) still call
      through one. Each converts to Effect, and the adapter goes with
      its last caller.
      The shallow faces went first: handlers answer with effects, and
      the root bridges its own callbacks (`process/captures.ts`). Left:
      the engine, the processes, the script runs, file-sync, and
      Terminals' one call from the scripts, by subsystem (projects and
      config, scripts, sync, mirror, the shell).
      Scripts went next, with the tombstone protocol and the deletes,
      moves and removals that run under it; sync's teardown keeps a
      Promise face for the delete until its own PR. Mirror and sync
      followed; what is left is projects and config, then the shell.
      Projects and config go in three: the engine calls as effects
      (`engineOps.ts`) behind a narrowed face (`engineCalls.ts`), then
      the worktrees and projects handlers, then sync's landing, moves
      and source link, the mirror sessions and registry and the control
      ops, the face going with the last. The handlers went on effects
      with the project and worktree lookups, then the face went with
      every host module that reached the engine through it. The Promise
      code left (the source link's protocol, the mirror's git follower,
      the background fetch) gets an engine handle from the effect or
      the root that starts it. The rest goes in three: git on Effect
      (`fromPromise` down from 65 calls to 31), then those Promise
      protocols with the engine handle (the follower and the fetch
      layers in the graph, peers reached through Effect clients;
      `fromPromise` down to 7), then the shell and the scaffolding, in
      two: every host handler and view an effect (the registrar's
      Promise path, the tracer's Promise face and `fromPromise` gone; a
      call that reaches a layer before it is up waits for it, and the
      listeners take the graph's services late from the root), then the
      shell's faces, the adapter and the root's captures. Done: the
      root reaches its graph through one door (`process/graph.ts`), a
      layer on top of the graph whose opening the listeners and the
      root's callbacks wait on, and publishes its pushes on a hub of
      its own; the shell's updater and link are layers of their own,
      and the adapter is gone.
- [x] A comment pass: the ordering and cleanup prose that the
      structure now enforces goes; the drifted comments (the version
      floor, fields by old names) get fixed.
- [x] Leftover mechanisms: what the Vocabulary replaces and the
      branch still uses, found by the maintainability review. Each
      goes with its last user, in this order (each frees the next):
      1. `implSlot` (`host/lib/util/implSlot.ts`, set at boot in
         `host/process/impls.ts`): `ipc/modules/runtime.ts`,
         `ipc/modules/portForward.ts`, `ipc/peerSync.ts`,
         `lib/control/peers.ts`, `mirror/registry.ts`,
         `process/shell.ts`, `process/facts.ts`. Each becomes a service
         in the host's graph, provided by the root, and `impls.ts` goes.
         The device id's setter (`host/lib/config/deviceId.ts`,
         `setDeviceId` from `main/hostProcess.ts` and the host's boot)
         is the same pattern and goes the same way.
         Done: `HostFacts` and `Shell` (what the shell hands over,
         provided by the root), `DeviceId` (read from the store as the
         graph comes up), `PeerReach`, `MirrorEngine` and `AccountPeers`
         (the root's wiring, `rootServices` in `process/handlers.ts`),
         `PortForwards` (a scoped layer whose closing stops every
         forward), and `DataDirHolders` for what a data-folder move
         lets go of. The runtime handlers push the nuke's progress
         through `HostPushes`. The shell takes the device id from its
         host's ready report, and a shell call that needs it waits for
         a host as long as the host's restart budget allows (a minute),
         then fails as it did before (`main/core/host/deviceId.ts`). The
         proofs stand the root's services in (`test/lib/hostStandIns.mts`),
         so a peer's handlers run on the peer's own mirror engine instead
         of swapping a slot. The root reading its facts off its command
         line (`process/facts.ts`) stays: it is the process boundary's
         one door, where the shell hands the utility process its facts,
         and everything below reads them as `HostFacts`.
         The setters at boot that never went through `implSlot` went the
         same way: what the mirror's serving side tells the root (the
         serving set, a served index, a follower's landing) is
         `MirrorSignals`, which the root provides; a script's start and
         end is `ScriptChanges`, which the root's changed push reads; a
         delete's removal is pushed from the handler (`HostPushes`); and
         the account the shell reports is `HostAccount`, which the
         root's callbacks read synchronously through the graph.
      2. `ttlCache` and `cacheTtl` (`host/lib/util`): `worktrees/carryOver`
         and `worktrees/hygiene` take a `Cache` in their service, as
         `GithubCli` and `Ports` do.
         Done: both are sub-makers of the host's `Worktrees` service
         (`host/lib/Worktrees.ts`), each read a `Cache` that keeps only
         answers (`cacheTtl.ts`). An answer's time to live now counts
         from when its read finished, not from when it was asked.
      3. `coalesce` (`host/lib/util/coalesce.ts`): the mirror and script
         change pushes in `process/handlers.ts` and
         `portForward/engine.ts` become a `Stream` at the source, one
         push a window opened by a burst's first change, as it is now.
         Done: each is a queue read as a `Stream`,
         `Stream.groupedWithin(Infinity, 150)`, forked in its owner's
         scope (the port forwards' engine, `mirrorChangesLayer`,
         `scriptsChangedLayer`). `Stream.throttle` would drop a burst's
         last change or space every change out. The mirror's list is
         now read one push at a time.
      4. `limit` and `singleFlight` (`shared/util`): the hub
         connection's lifecycle takes a `Semaphore`; the account
         handlers (`main/ipc/modules/account.ts`, `web/ipc/register.ts`)
         share a run in flight per call.
         Done for the account: an `Effect.cachedWithTTL` of zero per
         call, which shares a run in flight and keeps nothing after it
         (`Effect.cached` would keep the first answer for good). The hub
         connection's limiter went with its supervisor (6): its
         refreshes take a `Semaphore`, and `limit` with its last user.
      5. The cancel registry and its signal (`host/lib/util/abort.ts`,
         `sync/moves.ts`, `sync/landing.ts`): a move is a fiber the
         RPC call interrupts, and `sync:cancelMove` (the CLI's and a
         peer's mirror cancel by key) interrupts it through a
         `FiberMap` keyed by the move.
         Done: `sync/Moves.ts` runs each move on a fiber of a `FiberMap`
         its call waits on, so the call's interruption interrupts it,
         and a cancel by key fails the call with the cancel. A send's
         landing is cut short by its link's close, now a `Deferred`.
         `HandlerContext.signal` went with them.
      6. `supervisor` and `heartbeat` (`shared/remote/supervisor.ts`,
         `shared/hub/heartbeat.ts`): the hub connection (host, web,
         shared), the direct keeper and presence, cloudflared and the
         mirror daemon. Already fibers on `Schedule`s; what remains is
         their Promise face with `stop()`, which becomes a scoped layer
         per owner, and the hub socket's own ping in place of the
         heartbeat.
         Done: the supervisor, the hub connection, the direct keeper and
         the direct plane are scoped constructors, whose scope's close
         is their stop. The host makes the hub connection and the plane
         in one layer of its graph (`process/wires.ts`), and the web
         bridge in a scope its teardown closes. The hub socket's
         `HUB_PING` pings are fibers in the socket's own scope, with the
         same bytes and cadence, so `heartbeat.ts` went. cloudflared and
         the mirror daemon were already scoped layers, and keep only the
         supervisor's schedules.
      7. `atomicJson` (`host/lib/util/atomicJson.ts`): four files move
         into the store, as decision 5 names them: the running scripts

         `mirror-invites.json`, `process/handlers.ts`), so the nuke and

         `restartVisibility.json`, and `livenessRelaunch.json`, written

         Done for the store and the host: the running scripts and the
         mirror's agreements, history and invitations are the engine's

         `MirrorInvites`. A 2.x `git-follow.json`, `mirror-history.json`
         and `mirror-invites.json` are imported once, each by the
