      so never trips three in thirty and is recreated for good, as
      before; and the fatal handler's `app.exit` sets no shutdown flag,
      covered only by the destroyed window it leaves. The `host-restart`
      walk's "the whole app stopped … a commit sent as it stopped lands
      once" failed once in a local run of every walk on this build, with
      no logs kept, and passed alone and in the next full run with logs
      kept. That case is #984's (step 7's box on the `host-restart` walk:
      a commit's `git reset` refused `index.lock` by a view's read beside
      it, now asked again for 1.5 s), so, as with the `stashes` walk's
      flake, a second failure is that item come back, its cause read from
      its logs (a lock held past the retries, or another): a local `pnpm walks` now
      keeps a failed walk's root with the app's logs, as CI's
      `walk-logs` artifact does (`SM_WALK_FAILURES`, app/lab/walks.md).
      That keep's default, `app/lab/walks/failures/` inside the checkout,
      froze the owner's machine on 2026-10-18: a thread's walks run
      without `SM_WALK_FAILURES` kept 40 roots there (3 GB, about 85,000
      `.ts` files, each holding a copy of the repo), and its commit's
      typecheck read them all and grew past 22 GB on a 16 GB machine,
      twice, since gitignore hides nothing from the compiler. A root is
      kept now only under `SM_WALK_FAILURES`, never inside a checkout,
      this one or another (refused, said), every one of the run and the
      newest five of the runs before, pruned by their own names alone,
      and a keep or a prune that fails never masks the walk's error. Class: a gitignored folder inside the tree that a tool still
      reads. Checked by a probe file (a type error, a lint error, an
      unused export, a failing test) in each output a run writes inside
      the tree: the app's typecheck read the walks' old folder, the
      reliability harness's `report/` and the fake host's
      `villager-data/`, now excluded in `app/tsconfig.json`; oxlint,
      oxfmt and knip honour gitignore, and no vitest config reaches any of them
      (`.electron-dev`, `out`, `dist`, `dist-web`, `.vite`, the scene
      comparison's and the guide eval's reports, `coverage`, an engine
      `dist`, the hub's `.wrangler`, marketing's `dist`).
- [ ] Builds to dogfood and a nuke that works (the owner's addition),
      as designed under "The flavors" and "Nuke everything": the v3
      prereleases install beside the prod app, and nuke returns any
      build to a fresh install, so a first start can be tried again.
      - [x] Three flavors from one table (`packages/engine/src/flavor.ts`):
            prod, v3 and dev, each with its own name, bundle id,
            scheme, data dir, userData, logs, keychain item, `sm` and
            update line. The release workflow builds v3 for a
            `v3.0.0-alpha.N` or `v3.0.0-beta.N` tag, pointed at the
            dev hub (the `SM_V3_` variables, `SM_PREVIEW_` since the
            test flavors), and prod for anything else. The dogfood flavor
            was first named test; it is v3, Shigoto no Mori (v3),
            `com.sylophi.shigomori.v3`, `shigomori-v3://`, `~/.smv3`
            and `smv3`, for what it is.
      - [ ] When v3 merges into main, the v3 flavor goes: its row in
            the flavor table, the `SM_PREVIEW_` repo variables, and
            `shigomori-v3://app` in the dev Clerk instance's allowed
            origins.
      - [x] Test flavors, so a built app is tried in isolation and
            thrown away. `SM_FLAVOR=<word> pnpm package` builds a flavor
            whose names all follow from the word by the rule v3's fit
            (`flavor.ts` `derive`, and v3's row is checked against it):
            no updates, no login item, never Developer-ID signed (forge
            refuses), so the mock keychain, and the debugging port the
            packaged prod and v3 builds refuse, so weblab drives it as
            it drives the dev app (`app/scripts/smoke-app.mts --serve`).
            `footprint.ts` lists everything a flavor puts on the
            machine, and a proof checks that no test flavor's file,
            folder, keychain item, hook marker, hook command, bundle id
            or scheme is prod's, v3's or dev's. `<binary> nuke --yes` is
            its inverse with no window: the command quits the app,
            starts it with `--sm-nuke` to run the Danger zone's two
            halves and then the start that deletes the files (and a
            signed build's keychain item and its preferences), then
            removes its own folder, logs, preferences, the config folder
            once empty, and the bundle (`--keep-app` keeps it). On a
            `lab` build here: the smoke, `--serve` with weblab attached
            to its window, then `smlab nuke --yes`, after which none of
            the footprint's paths, the bundle or a process of it
            remained. Still shared between flavors, as no test needs
            them apart: the in-project worktree folder `.shigomori/wt`
            (a layout a project opts into), the repo refs under
            `refs/shigomori/` (keyed by worktree id), the
            `$TMPDIR/shigomori-pr-attachments` and `sm-index-*` temp
            files, and terrier's registry and port-pool's leases. The
            release job's hub and Clerk variables are `SM_PREVIEW_*`
            (were `SM_V3_*`): every build before prod's points there.
            The first packaged check before a cut (alpha.8, on a data
            folder under /tmp) found two things. Creating a worktree
            compared git's listing with its own path textually, and git
            spells a path with its symlinks resolved, so under a data
            folder reached through a symlink (/tmp, or a data dir linked
            to another drive) it refused with "worktree disappeared
            after creation" for a worktree it had made, and its listing
            read every worktree there as external (no ports, teardown or
            shelving), and the doctor every one as a stray. Creation now
            takes either spelling, as a move already did, the managed
            bases are listed in both, and the doctor scans each base
            once, as git spells it (`worktrees/creating.ts`,
            `Layout.ts`, `doctor/projectChecks.ts`,
            `Worktrees.create.test.ts`). v2
            has the same comparison (the Go `sm`'s `cli/worktree.go`,
            which the v2 app's create also goes through) and is left as
            it is. And nuke cleared a login item on a test flavor, which
            never sets one, so macOS logged "Unable to set login item:
            Operation not permitted"; nuke and the launch-at-login
            reconcile now share the rule of which builds set one
            (`liveness.ts` `setsLoginItem`).
      - [x] Nuke everything, complete and resumable: a live half in the
            host and the shell, then the files at the next start before
            anything opens them (`host/lib/nuke.ts`,
            `main/electron/nuke.ts`, `main/core/nuke/finish.ts`), held by
            `pnpm test nuke`. Seen end to end on the dev flavor
            (Measurements). The terminal has no verb for it.
      - [x] A packaged build's relaunch keeps its window. `app.relaunch()`
            started the new instance from a relauncher the old app spawned,
            so macOS counted it among the old app's subordinate processes,
            and a second after a foreground app quits, loginwindow
            terminates its subordinates unless Background Task Management
            says it may run in the background (its log: "applicationDeath:
            ... still has subordinate processes ... scheduling its
            subordinates' termination"). Every child of the new instance
            went (the window's renderer, the GPU, the host), which left a
            dead window after the migration's restart, a data-folder move
            or a nuke. The app now starts again through LaunchServices
            once it has exited, as `sm update` brings it back
            (`main/electron/relaunch.ts`). `node lab/relaunch.mts <app>`
            holds it on a packaged build: it failed before the change and
            passes after.
      - [x] The v3 flavor signed in beside dev, both on the hub, and
            its signed-in nuke run (Measurements): enrolled as its own
            device beside a dev profile, each listing the other, then
            revoked by the nuke and back on the onboarding signed out.
            Since #806 a packaged build quits when asked for a debugging
            port, so the run drove the v3 window through macOS
            accessibility. `lab/relaunch.mts` does the same now: the
            app, started with `--sm-lab`, relaunches on `SIGUSR2`, and
            the lab reads the new window through accessibility and the
            killed children in `main.log` (passes on a local v3 build:
            the window drawn 10 s after the ask, 72 texts and buttons
            5 s later; a window whose renderer was killed reads 3).
      - [ ] The login item, on the first signed v3 prerelease: the local
            build is signed ad hoc, and macOS refuses it a login item
            ("Unable to set login item: … Operation not permitted" on
            its stderr, from `~/Applications` too), while Settings still
            reads Keep this device reachable as on.
- [ ] The five review passes, the `v3.0.0-alpha.N` and `-beta.N`
      prereleases on every
      device, `v3.0.0-rc.1`, merge, tag. Remove this file.
      Each pass ends with a report to the orchestration thread, and one
      that lands findings adds a line to "What the audits found and
      where it goes".
      - [x] Regressions from v2, found three ways. In code: main's
            behaviour walked area by area against its v3 counterpart,
            with the parity harness and the golden fixtures as the
            baseline. In builds: a v2 build and a v3 build on copies of
            the same data and the same devices, every page and flow on
            both, shots side by side in both themes. In quality: a new
            feature of lower quality than the pages it was added to
            counts as a regression, the UI above all. Evidence: the
            areas walked with what differed, and the paired shots.
            Walked on origin/main 61550b563 against release/v3
            8e68b43f8, then each fix on the branch as it moved.
            In code, every area of v2 against v3: the renderer's routes
            and pages, the menus, context menus, dialogs and Settings,
            notifications, deep links and the updater's UI, the palette,
            keys, toasts and sidebar, theme and village life, the web
            client, the host's features and file-sync, and the terminal
            `sm` (100+ command lines of both binaries diffed). Matching
            v2: the menus, the context menus but the native text menu's
            new library, every dialog (the create-PR and hard-reset ones
            included), every v2 toast, theme, palettes and village life,
            the host's features, file-sync's verbs, every v2 IPC channel.
            In builds: a packaged v2 is the prod flavor (the prod hub, the
            installed app's data), so v2 ran as a dev build of main beside
            a v3 dev build on fresh profiles of one repo; the dev hub's
            floor refuses v2 ("too old"), so v2's signed-in flows could

            screens. First run, a project added, a worktree made, its

            request's page (a real one), Settings and the terminal. A v3
            package was built and inspected, not run (the flavors thread
            ran its first run and nuke). In quality: every screen of the
            fake host, desktop at 920×720 and phone at 390×844, light and
            dark, v2 and v3 side by side (fixture data aside, every
            existing screen matched); v3's new screens (welcome, the
            migration's states, Keyboard, the guide's parts) held to them.
            The paired shots are kept outside the repo, 197 named pairs.
            Found and closed:
            - High: the file icons were neither served nor packaged since
              #794 (#832); an edit outside the app never reached the
              worktree rows, which re-read only on store writes and ref
              moves (#834); the account page's banner button did nothing,
              so a lapsed web session could only sign out (#835); an
              offline peer's projects vanished from every surface (#837).
            - Medium: a failed read of the settings, shared settings or
              projects said nothing, and Save wrote defaults over stored
              values (#835); the client config cached per window, so one
              window's write reverted another's (#835); a never-reached
              peer kept the sidebar loading for good (#837); the script
              console's size and focus at a run's start, and its 280px
              drawer on a phone (#837); every phone footer label folding
              at once (#838); the terminal `sm`'s parsing (single-dash
              longs, dash values, last flag wins, bare booleans, `wt -p`,
              extra positionals), `disk-usage`, lifecycle scripts' color,
              land's order, the rm --stack hint, the merge queue note, the
              agent bind on every verb (#840); a mirror into a copy the
              wt/ move re-keyed ended on upgrade (#842); ⌘K by physical
              key and extra modifiers no longer matching (#843).
            - Low: the updater's in-flight guard, Clerk telemetry against
              the CSP, the welcome card's band (#838); the closed-PR box's
              offline note (#837); the description's and reword's ⌘↩ off
              the keymap, the menu's keys before a window sends its own
              (#843); the CLI's merge and color lows (#840).
            Not regressions after a look: the create-PR pickers (they
            mount in the dialog's portal, themed), the empty home's lone
            button (#765 designed it), Update all's fallback for pre-v3
            peers.
            Left, and why:
            - With the owner: the empty home's explanation of the app.

              with the row, the owner's call.
            - The update toasts show once per window: a shared claim, one

            - The data-folder move re-keys worktrees without a move row,

            - A dev run moves `~/.local/bin/smd` and `shigomori-dev` to

            - The "Refreshing worktree state…" spin: a live view has no

            - The terminal `sm`: JSON key order and escaping,

      - [x] Robustness, which must increase from v2 and be high across

            every tagged error reaches the user or the trace with its
            cause, every crash and restart path resumes, and the
            proofs' fakes cover the faults the soaks found. Evidence:
            the soaks in Measurements, and v2's and v3's outcomes per
            scenario.
            Done: the error-path review (below, #845 to #859); the
            soaks in Measurements; and the local failure scenarios run
            on v3 and on v2 by hand, side by side (Measurements,
            "Failure paths in the lab"), v3 the same as v2 where git
            refuses and better where the app had to read or recover (a
            host killed mid-commit, an unreadable index, a drive gone).
            The hub-bound scenarios are not comparable: the dev hub
            refuses v2.
            The proofs that failed under load now wait on what they
            assert, not on how fast the machine is: `control`'s "mirror
            --from" was the terminal's own bug, its ping limit the ping
            interval (Effect's default), so a call the app took over 5 s
            to answer was usually cut, now 15 s silent (#845);
            cancelMove read a pid file the shell had made but not yet
            written; the scenes render with no per-case deadline, since
            they wait on nothing; the hub's budget case sends one past
            the burst on a stopped clock. With them, a menu with `CI`
            set in the environment drew nothing while it waited for
            keys. Left: `shell-files`' updater bridge (once on CI, its
            log gone): the bridge's watch does start late (the
            platform's starts on a fiber of its own, after a stat), but
            macOS's file events reach a watcher made just after the
            write, and 12 runs under load pass; and the cli picker's two
            CI failures, one the binary dying with "EBADF: bad file
            descriptor, fstat" as ink first read stdin, the other a key
            lost after the menu took input. With the fd 0 trace (#861)
            showing fd 0 a tty, both came back here under six busy
            loops, about one run in sixty, and most likely have one
            cause, in Bun: its process.stdin dups fd 0 when a reader
            first attaches and keeps that fd for every later reader, and
            the dup takes the lowest free fd number. The menu attached
            first, and the fd table showed that number was one the git
            children the command had just run were giving back, their
            stdio sockets closing as the menu attached. That fd was then
            closed under the reader: EBADF at the reader's fstat, or,
            traced in the binary, a reader that never woke while the
            key's three bytes sat in the terminal's queue (FIONREAD)
            with ink listening. Which close it is wasn't caught (no
            syscall tracing here), so the cause is likely, not proven.
            The bound (`packages/cli/src/stdinFd.ts`): the binary holds
            an fd from its start, before any child runs, and as the menu
            attaches gives it back and has Bun make its dup in one
            synchronous step, so the dup takes a number no child's pipe
            can have had. Nothing reads stdin before a menu shows, so
            typeahead stays the shell's (a first try that started the
            reader at startup swallowed it, which the review caught; a
            case now holds it). With the give-back a step before ink's
            own attach, one run in 960 still lost its key; in one step,
            960 runs under the same load, none lost. It is a workaround
            for Bun: a Bun bump runs `pnpm -C packages/cli under-load`
            (every at-a-terminal case repeated beside busy loops, eight
            rounds) before it lands.
            The shell's hold on its host (#846): a quit asks whether the
            host is busy for 3 s at most, and one that cannot say is
            quit with no prompt (the busy check behind "Restart to
            update" the same), and the graph's close at a quit is bounded
            at 20 s, so a quit or a logout never waits on a host that
            does not answer (v2 had a 15 s backstop; v3 waited for good);
            a host that answers nothing for 30 s is killed and forked
            again, within the restart budget (v3 restarted only a host
            that exited); and a host that came back is dialed once, where
            a reconnect asked as it came up closed the shell's first link
            to it and could lose the account it was told
            (`pnpm test host-hold`). On a dev profile with its host
            stopped (SIGSTOP): the watchdog forked a new host 40 s in;
            a quit exited in 5.4 s, where it had waited for good (the
            host that did not answer the busy check is killed, not
            waited on to close).
            And this machine's own device reads "Not responding" in rose
            (its tab, its filter, its live card) while the window's link
            to its host is down, from the host stopping or the watchdog
            stopping it until the next one answers, the one place a hung
            host shows (#857, the owner's yes; `app/DESIGN.md`'s device
            line, the `marks` scene, `pnpm test host-hold`).
            Every account report from the shell reconciles the hub socket
            again, as v2's did (#847): Retry enrollment on a blocked
            socket enrolled the device again under the same account,
            with a new credential and key that the socket, refreshed
            only on a change of account, hub or web origin, never saw,
            so it stayed blocked, and a socket the hub had closed for
            the rotation came back on the new key while its sealed
            handshakes used the old one (`pnpm test hub-refresh`).
            `land`, `rm --stack` and `done` keep the commits a pull
            request never had (#848): each deleted its branch with
            `branch -D` once the PR (or one of that name) was merged,
            so a commit made after the last push, or work on a branch
            name an older PR had merged with, went with it, in v2 as in
            v3. Each now reads the PR's head and refuses, naming the
            commits (`unpushed-commits` in `--json`), unless `-f` or
            `--keep-branch`, and `done` says when GitHub couldn't be
            asked rather than calling the branch unmerged.
            A data-folder move a crash cut short is finished or undone
            at the next start (#849): the move re-keyed every worktree
            under the folder before copying it to another volume, so a
            crash mid-copy left the store keyed to paths that never came
            to be (their titles, marks and ports gone from view) and the
            partial copy refused every retry, in v2 as in v3. The move
            now keeps a journal from before its re-key to its end and
            copies into a sibling renamed in once whole; a start that
            finds the journal where the pointer still leads undoes the
            re-key and removes the sibling, and one that finds it at the
            destination finishes the cleanup (`pnpm test data-dir-move`
            stops the copy halfway, never resumes it, and starts again).
            A re-key that failed partway is undone too, since the undo no
            longer waits on the whole re-key having succeeded, and the
            engine's re-key moves nothing from an id that holds nothing,
            where it had wiped the target's title and ports. A copy
            renamed in before the pointer moved is removed with the
            undo, by the journal it carries.
            Three links that stayed quiet when they failed (#854): a host
            view the host answered with its own failure (its read of the
            store failing, a `RemoteCallError`) was asked again every
            two seconds for good, unseen, where v2 retried three times
            and said so; it now ends, and the page's error shows. One
            frame the host's link couldn't read left its parser unable
            to read the next, so the link stayed up answering nothing
            until the dialer's ping gave up; the connection now closes
            (1007) and is dialed again. And a hub that refused this
            device's ticket (any 401 or 403, a misdeployed hub's for
            every device) blocked it until the next sign-in, against its
            own comment; it is asked again two minutes on.
            A git or gh that stalls ends, and offline isn't signed out
            (#852): one `git fetch` of a project left half open (a sleep,
            a network that changed) froze its background fetch, its
            auto-pull and its sync and merge buttons until a restart,
            since every caller shares the one in flight; network git now
            ends under 1 KB/s for a minute, and the shared fetch after
            five minutes, its git with it. The engine's gh, which the
            app's merge runs through, had no deadline of its own; it has
            two minutes now. And gh read as signed out whenever `gh auth
            status`, which asks GitHub, failed (offline, a stale second
            account); readiness reads `gh auth token`, gh's own store.
            A move's source answers every ask (#853): a step on its
            files that threw (a temporary folder it couldn't make, a full
            disk mid-bundle) ended its serving without a word, and the
            other device waited for good on the answer; every failure is
            now answered in words, defects included, the capture's and
            the bundle's file steps fail as errors in errno words, and a
            pull whose source can't be asked after it landed completes
            with a note about the files rather than failing a worktree
            that is there (`sync-transfer` points TMPDIR nowhere during
            a pull).
            Two landing paths that read as done when they weren't
            (#850): a stack land on a repo without GitHub's stacks API
            merged each layer with `gh pr merge`, which exits 0 on a PR a
            merge queue only queued, then retargeted the next layer onto
            the trunk and removed every worktree; it now reads each layer
            back and stops at one that hasn't merged, as a queued stack
            does, and a merge whose read-back fails is waited on, never
            taken as merged. And a land of the primary checkout, or a
            `done`, whose pull failed after the merge failed whole, the
            merged branch kept for good (a land again looked up the
            primary's PR); the branch now goes before the pull, and a
            failed pull is said as a skipped catch-up.
            The engine's and the terminal's failures in words, with no
            hang and no lock a reading start needs not take (#851): a
            failure of the store's SQL reads by what to do (busy, the
            disk full, read-only, damaged: `StoreError`, `store-*` codes)
            where it read "Failed to execute statement", and the store's
            open says the same; a start runs the migrator only when a
            migration is due, and the `wt/` scan only when one is owed,
            so `sm list` answers while the app writes, where it failed
            behind the write lock; `sm run` and a launcher's open don't
            fail on the usage log; a folder deleted under the app reads
            as gone, not as git or gh missing; the update staging lock
            of a pid the system handed to another process after a crash
            or a reboot is stale, a lock that can't be written says why,
            and the doctor's repair goes through the lock; the doctor's
            commands, the updater's (a headless install among them) and
            the store's boot report end past a deadline; and a commit cut
            short before it committed puts the index back.
            The shell recovers where it gave up (#855): a host that
            kept stopping put up a dialog even on a machine kept
            reachable, where nobody may be there to dismiss it; it now
            relaunches within the fatal-relaunch budget there, and only
            a store that won't open, or a spent budget, keeps the
            dialog. The shared settings' writer ended on the first write
            the store refused (busy past the driver's five seconds, the
            disk full), and every change after it was announced and
            never stored; it tries again until the write lands, the
            changes after it in order (`pnpm test settings-busy-store`).
            And a data folder moved to a drive that isn't connected
            started a host that failed to make it, behind a dialog that
            said the host couldn't start (9 s in the lab); the start now
            says the folder's drive isn't connected, through links to
            it too, with the pointer to delete for the default, before
            any host is forked (`pnpm test unmounted-volume`).
            Proof kit, left: every proof file runs the host runtime,
            the shared settings' writer among it, so a proof that builds
            the settings layer again on a sandbox engine
            (`shared-settings.mts`'s host copy) runs two writers on one
            queue, each storing to its own engine; harmless to its
            asserts today, but a case that needs the one writer runs on
            the host runtime's store (`settings-busy-store`).
            A mirror outlives an unmounted volume, and a failing engine
            says why (#856, protocol 13): a mirror whose original sat
            on a drive that was unplugged or asleep read "no such file"
            and was ended as an original deleted, in v2 as in v3; a root
            under a `/Volumes` mount that isn't there now waits for the
            drive (`mirror.mts`). A file-sync engine that kept exiting
            read as Restarting for good, and one that was there but
            wouldn't spawn read as missing; the engine now reads as
            failing, with its exit code and its last word on stderr, or
            why it couldn't start, until it is ready again: a rose
            "Engine failing" on the mirror, the start buttons' refusal,
            and `sm mirrors` (`mirror-daemon-lines`, `mirror-status`).
            Protocol 13 carries it (a new status literal, and the reason
            in `mirror:list` and `control:mirrors`), with #834's
            follow-up: `worktrees:reread` is gone, and `git:sweep` says
            whether it is a renewal. The dev hub deploys with it.
            The failure paths in the lab (#861), on a dev profile at
            protocol 13 (Measurements, "Failure paths in the lab"): a
            failing hook, a held index lock and a full disk each say
            git's own words under the commit form, keep the summary,
            and commit on the next click once the cause is gone; a host
            killed mid-commit is back in 0.2 s and its git finishes the
            commit; a project's drive unplugged reads as "is not there
            anymore" on a refresh and comes back with the drive. Two
            finds, fixed here: the changes page kept its last read
            through an edit made while the window had the focus (the
            app's own terminal, an agent), behind the row's own count,
            and now reads again when the row moves; and a read git could
            no longer answer (an index it can't read) left the last
            answer standing, "No changes" on a tree with an edit, where
            it now says it couldn't read them (`pnpm test changes-state`).
            On a peer's page the list follows the row's count and newest
            change, which an edit to a file already listed under an
            untracked folder moves neither of; it follows on the next
            focus. Left: following it there means `worktrees:
            filesChanged` on the remote surface, every edit fanned out
            to the account's peers, for the owner to weigh.
            Left, for the next protocol bump: the worktree's row counts
            a status git refused as no changes (the listing's choice, so
            the shelf compares nothing), so the worktree page and the
            sidebar still say "No uncommitted changes" over an
            unreadable index; saying so needs a field on the row.
            The data folder's leftovers (#863): a move asked here
            that failed after the host let go of the folder left the
            app running without its loopback and store watch, a toast
            its only word; the shell now says what failed with one
            button, Restart, and dismissing it quits, so no such app is
            left running (the owner's call). A nuke's worktree removal
            no longer waits for good on a teardown script that never
            ends: it gives each a minute, then removes the worktree
            without it (`pnpm test nuke`, `data-dir-move`). Left: a
            move to another volume copies the store while the host can
            still write it (a setting changed mid-copy is lost to the
            moved folder); a copy through SQLite's own backup would
            close it, for a later pass.
            Left, the pass's lows, each small enough to wait for the
            next person in that code: the git step missing from the
            terminal's errors (v2 printed `git pull …:`);
            `mergeSettings` swallowing gh failures; the merge wait
            unbounded while a check it waits on is visible; the staging
            lock's errno lost; the doctor reading ok when macfs fails
            (v2 better); the disk-usage walk with no timeout; empty
            spawn-failure causes in the updater's messages; `projects
            add`'s best-effort steps silent; a script file's errors
            losing the reason (v2 better); forget and rekey not in one
            transaction; `projects relocate` not resumable; the orphan
            sweep's row ordering; a missing path read as "not a git
            repository"; a move's rollback that failed still saying
            "left as it was"; link errors worded "The other device
            answered: …"; a follow pull leaving a landing ref behind;
            v2-era mirror ignores refusing a submodule's `.git`; "no
            commits yet" for every git read failure; the post-nuke
            cleanup throwing at start; "remote device disconnected"
            untagged for a local host; the account's HTTP calls with no
            deadline; `sm devices` and `send` hanging on a stalled hub;
            every peer probe failure printed as "not connected"; peer
            calls with no limit (48 stuck calls block the rest); a data
            folder move dropping the worktrees' terminals; Ports' and
            GithubCli's `orDie`; an old gh blanking the PR list
            silently; `send`'s help saying it "finishes there even if
            interrupted"; and the updater bridge's watch starting after
            it returns.
            `direct-plane`'s ticket expiry case failed once more on CI
            (#850's first run), its 80 ms ticket outlived by a slow dial:
            the tickets now run on the case's own clock.
      - [x] Security, which must increase from v2 and be tight across
            the board. Done: the hub audit (below) and the sealed
            link's two reviews (#766, #769; the end-to-end encryption
            box). Left: the desktop shell, the CLI control wire, the
            file-sync daemon, the web client's storage and CSP, the
            consent table and the command gates, secrets handling, the
            supply chain (and which of main's Dependabot alerts survive
            v3's lockfile), and the guide, each by fresh reviewers and
            then a second reviewer. Evidence: the findings with their
            fixes, and a written comparison of v2's posture with v3's.
            Reviewed by a fresh reviewer, then a second (Opus, reading
            the code and Mutagen v0.18.1's source), each finding
            confirmed by running it where it could be: the desktop
            shell, the host and its loopback with the gates and the
            consent table, the terminal `sm` and its control wire,
            file-sync, the web client, secrets in logs and traces, the
            supply chain, main's ports (#777, #778, #788, #789, #790)
            and the guide (#800). Clean: the windows' sandbox and
            isolation, `openExternal`, deep links, the fuses, the
            single-instance lock, the shell hook and agent hooks, the
            web client's storage and CSP, secrets (no token, key,
            ticket or credential in a log, a trace or an error), and the
            guide's tokens, debugger and reach (the window alone, our
            own page functions, no port).
            Found and closed, first review:
            - High: a peer's mirror stream chose its root, so a peer
              invited to mirror one worktree, with commands off, could
              read, write or delete anywhere on the serving machine; v2
              has the same (#793).
            - Medium: commands turned off left a peer's open views
              running (#793); the store, holding terminal history, was
              readable by the machine's other accounts (#793); an update
              was checked by its Team ID alone, so another flavor's or an
              older release passed (#794); the window had no CSP (#794);
              all twelve of main's Dependabot alerts survived in the
              lockfile, Electron 43.1.0 among them (#795).
            - Low: a wrong loopback token locked out the loopback's own
              callers, and was compared in time that varied (#793).
            Found and closed, second review:
            - High: the mirror stream still chose its session id, which
              Mutagen joins into paths it writes and removes, where it
              staged, and whether .git crossed, and could write under
              .git; file-sync now serves its own copy of Mutagen's
              endpoint server under the host's rules (#805).
            - Medium: a granted call's pushes (a script's console)
              outlived both switches; any loopback-token holder reached
              the shell's session calls, which set the host's account,
              now behind a token the shell alone holds (#806); a staged
              release could go over a newer app installed since, and the
              bundle's version and identifier were not required of it
              (#809); the guide could click the access switches, whose
              control has no name, now declared with
              `data-users-alone` and held by a ui test (#813).
            - Low: the command gate read its switch before subscribing,
              the Clerk token file was 0644, a packaged build accepted
              remote debugging (#806); a Makefile target named like one
              of make's options was listed (#813).
            Decided by the owner, then closed:
            - Markdown images from any host told a PR's author who
              viewed it. They load from GitHub and the hosts of the
              projects' remotes alone, an enterprise GitHub's
              subdomains included, inline styles go, and the window's
              CSP `img-src` names the same hosts (#821).
            - The guide could run code from the app with its tools
              pre-approved (a script's Run, the launch tools, a script's
              or a launcher's command field): they carry
              `data-users-alone` too, held by the ui test, and the guide
              takes no text on a marked control (#822).
            Left open, and why:
            - An enterprise GitHub's images in the web client: its
              markdown follows the same rule, but its CSP is a static
              header (`app/vercel.json`) that can't name a user's hosts,
              and opening it to any https host would loosen it for
              everyone. (Closed since: such an image is read by the
              project's device and shown as a data: URL, the
              retrospective's O02.)
            - Exact pins in the catalog: not done, since the lockfile
              pins and the cooldown and ignore-scripts guard the
              install.
            - Three proofs that fail under load and pass alone, waiting
              for a deadline that fits or a `waitFor`
              (`app/test/README.md`): `control`'s "mirror --from" (2 of
              about 14 runs on a loaded machine, "Lost the connection to
              the app"; none in 9 before #806 and at it),
              `sync-transfer`'s cancelMove (pull) ("no setup pid"), and
              `shell-files`' updater bridge (once in CI on #821, "timed
              out waiting for the request to be consumed").
            v2 against v3: v3 adds end-to-end encryption on every
            device transport, a host reached only over a token-guarded
            loopback, a classification golden file with gates that
            fail closed, the sharing switch and scoped invitations, the
            hub's caps and bounds (#749), and everything above; its new
            surface (terminals, three flavors under one Team ID, the
            web device key, the guide) is covered by it. Nothing v2
            held was lost.
            The hub audit, from step 5:
            - [x] Kind comes from the client-reported platform, so any
                  same-account device can claim web and hold many
                  sockets. No per-device socket cap exists (DO cost
                  only). Capped: a device holds at most 32 sockets
                  whatever its kind (#749).
            - [x] connectInfo answers, with direct tickets, reach every
                  tab of a web device. Sealed to the device through the
                  relay; the tickets are kept per connection, each spent
                  only by its authenticated hello (the end-to-end
                  encryption box).
            - [x] GET /connect is unauthenticated, guarded by HMAC
                  tickets. Kept: each ticket is bound to the credential
                  that minted it, voided by a re-enrollment (#749), and
                  checked as the socket opens (#766).
            - [x] Rate limiting fails open, keyed on CF-Connecting-IP.
                  Unchanged. A socket's messages are now bounded in size
                  before parsing and in rate, a burst of 300 and 30 a
                  second, ten times the measured peak of an account at
                  its cap (#749).
            - [x] Any login evicts the account's stalest offline device
                  when full. Kept: enrollment is admitted in the
                  account's Durable Object, so concurrent ones cannot
                  pass the cap (#749).
            - [x] CORS is `*` with bearer-only auth. Unchanged.
            - [x] The version header is client-asserted, not security.
                  Unchanged.
            - [x] tunnel.ts keeps a legacy-width teardown. Gone (#749):
                  a device cannot know its old tunnel's id; such
                  tunnels are idle configuration, removed from the
                  dashboard.
            - [x] A window's host relays a peer's view
                  (`hub:watchPeer`) with no bound, so a flood the window
                  cannot keep up with is buffered in the host. Bounded at
                  64 values a view, past which the view ends as no
                  session and the window asks again (the reliability
                  pass).
            - [x] A tunnel's provision answers success while its
                  connector keeps the first ingress it was given
                  (config version 1, found by the reliability pass). The
                  Worker now reads the ingress back and fails a
                  provision whose rewrite did not take, logging both
                  versions (#873). Deployed to the dev hub and tailed
                  through a host restart onto a new port of the very
                  tunnel that had stayed on version 1: the provision
                  read its new ingress back (no mismatch logged), and
                  the connector routed to the new port 3 s after the
                  restart. The stale version 1 came while Cloudflare's
                  edge was also refusing fresh tunnels (15:06 to 15:30
                  PDT on 2026-10-12), and has not come back. A host
                  now keeps its port as well (#870).
            #749 also closes a re-enrolled device's sockets and turns
            invocation logs off.
      - [x] Maintainability: fresh reviewers against the branch's own
            rules (`EFFECT.md`, `app/DESIGN.md`, the folder READMEs)

            small task in each package with only the docs. Evidence:
            the findings, and where each agent's task went wrong.

            - ui: the scenes proof one case per scene; how to add a scene

            The practical test, twelve fresh agents with only the docs,
            all done, every check green: contracts 15 min, engine 6, cli
            3, ui 4, app/host 13, app/main 7, app/shared 2, renderer 15,
            app/web 9, app/lab 8, hub 7 to 10, marketing 3. Where they went
            wrong was the docs, not the frameworks: the empty

            Each is a fix above. Left open: the Leftover mechanisms box,

            Protocol 11: a byte channel's refused open crosses as
            `ChannelRefusedError` (its reason a field), which the
            renderer branches on by tag, in place of a marker matched in
            the message; the hub deploys with it. Protocol 12: main's
            #789 and #790 carried in, `worktrees:describe` and
            `packageScripts:list` answering with the scripts grouped by
            file (`groups`) and a `packageManager` of null when there is
            no package.json. Main's #799 and #802 carried in on 12: new
            calls (`worktrees:resetHard`, the `githubCli` calls that
            create a pull request) and an optional `countsOnly` on
            `worktrees:mergePreview`, which an older build answers or
            ignores. Main's #892 carried in on 13: a new call
            (`launchers:openFile`, `remote: false` like `launch`), an
            optional `editor` on a detected launcher, and an `editor`
            device setting, a launch-catalog key a peer's settings patch
            never carries.
            Protocol 14: main's #894 and #896 carried in. The files
            page's read (`worktrees:readFile`) answers an image, video or
            audio file with a new `media` member, which an older build
            can't decode, up to 16 MiB as on main, its base64 in one
            frame that every link now decodes up to 32 MiB
            (`LINK_MAX_FRAME_BYTES`, effect's default 16); a PR's detail
            gains an optional `bodySource`; and `worktrees:describe` edits the open PR
            through this device's gh, its annotations unchanged (gated,
            `changeCode`).
            Main's #897 carried in on 14, no contract change: every gh
            run in a repo, the engine's (`GitHub.run`) and the host's
            (`githubCli/exec.ts`), is pinned with `GH_HOST` to the host
            of the remote gh resolves the repo from (`GitHub.hostOf`,
            over `ghHost.ts`).
            Left: the host lookup runs before the gh deadline (a
            stalled checkout waits past it; the fix is the `hostOf`
            yield under the timeout), and gh runs outside a repo (the
            sign-in probe, owner and repository listings, `gh repo
            create`, the doctor) go to gh's default host, as on main.
            Main's #901 carried in on 14, a new call and no bump: an
            image, video or audio file in a diff shows its old and new
            sides. Each side is `worktrees:readBlob` (remote, ungated
            like the diffs), answered with the files page's
            `WorktreeFileSchema` as it already was on 14, so a peer on an
            older build answers it with a defect for that call alone; the
            diff views (`watchFileDiff` and the patch reads) stay a
            string. The blob reads are the engine's Git
            (`Git.readBlob`, `Git.isWorkingTreeBlob`, over a byte-reading
            `runBytes` beside `run`), the working-tree patch prints full
            ids (`--full-index`), and the host's `readGitBlob`
            (`host/lib/worktrees/files.ts`) picks the cap and the kind.
            Main's media table moved into the contracts
            (`mediaTypes.ts`), which the host and the diff page both
            read; the sides draw through `MediaDiffView` in a new
            `media` slot of `DiffFileRowView`, reusing `MediaFileView`, and
            a side is a React Query request (a blob never changes), read
            once scrolled near. One frame carries one side: each answer
            is held to `WORKTREE_MEDIA_MAX_BYTES` (16 MiB, about 21.4 MiB
            as base64 with its envelope) and is its own response, under
            `LINK_MAX_FRAME_BYTES` (32 MiB), so nothing changed there;
            both sides in one answer would have run past it. Checked:
            typecheck, lint, format, knip, the contracts suite with the
            classification and the 14 samples (the new call added), the
            engine and ui suites, `diff-media` ported to vitest, and the
            proofs the change reaches (80 files). Left: the sides were
            rendered by the ui suite's scenes test only, not looked at
            in the lab or the app.
            Main's #902, #903 and #904 carried in on 14, no bump: an
            agent's session end keeps its binding (the engine's
            `applyAgentEvent` in `packages/engine/src/agentSessions.ts`
            sets `SessionEnd` idle like `Stop`, only `SubagentStop`
            unbinds; the Go CLI's change went there and to the `agents
            event` help), a branch's only commit can be undone
            (`commitRewriteAt`'s `below` in
            `packages/ui/src/lib/commitRewrite.ts`, merged as it was),
            and the doubutsu palettes rotate. The rotation adds
            `themeRotation` and `themeRotatedAt` to `ClientConfigSchema`
            (`packages/contracts/src/schemas/config.ts`), two optional
            fields on a plain struct, and two members to its `clear`
            literals, which the README's letter calls a bump; the module
            is client-scoped (`modules/clientConfig.ts`, not `remote`),
            so the only decoder is the same build's shell or web tab, as
            `welcomed` went in on 7 with #765. The logic sits in
            `packages/ui/src/lib/themes.ts`, the toggle and interval in
            `AppearanceSectionView`, and the renderer's
            `lib/themeRotation.ts` is written for v3: the launch turn
            reads the shell's config through `clientConfigOnce` (a 5 s
            give-up, where main's read had none), the minute check
            through `clientConfigNow`, and a turn is a
            `patchClientConfig` the window shows at once and reverts if
            the patch fails. Checked: typecheck, lint, format, knip, the
            contracts, engine, ui and cli suites, the `theme-picks` and
            `client-config` proofs, and in the lab the appearance rows
            in both schemes and a real Settings save with the twin pairs
            and a launch turn. Left, inherited from main: a launch turn
            runs per window that boots, so a second window on "Each
            launch" turns the pair, and two windows' minute checks can
            turn twice; the v3 shape is one turn per launch by the
            config's owner (`main/electron/clientConfig.ts`), its own
            item. The Settings hold stays, though v3's form reseeds and
            saves only the touched fields, because it keeps the swatches
            still while they are looked at. The comment on
            `CommitRewrite` still points at the app's copy and
            `theme-picks.mts`'s header names only the picks, neighbouring
            fixes for the simplification pass.
            Main's #918 carried in on 15, no contract change: on a
            phone, a tab page lays a thin fixed strip along the top edge
            in the colour it opens on, which Safari 26 on iOS tints its
            status bar from (else from the body, the card a stacked
            page's back bar wants). The strip is `AppShellView`'s
            `statusBarEdge`, which `AppShell` sets on a tab route, so a
            scene's picture of a window draws none; `PageHeaderView`
            carries `data-slot="page-header"` for doubutsu.css's band
            colour; main's index.css comment went onto `usePageCanvas`,
            where v3 paints the phone canvas. Checked: `pnpm check`,
            `pnpm walks` on a lab build, and the web fake host at
            390x844 in WebKit (the band on Settings, the canvas on Inbox,
            none on a stacked page). Left: not seen on an iPhone. For
            the renderer pass, seen during this port and not from it: a
            full-page goto in the web fake host logs a "registry is
            disposed" pageerror from `remoteSweep.ts:121`.
            The blob read takes a full id only (`ReadBlobPayloadSchema`,
            40 or 64 hex), so a peer reads only what a diff showed it.
            Every patch the host makes for a diff page carries
            `--full-index` (`fileDiff`, `commitDiff`, `mergeBaseDiff` and
            `readStashDiff`, each proven by `diff-media`), and
            `readGitBlob` hands git's bytes to `classify` without a copy.
            No bump: no build had shipped the call.
            Left, the owner's call: the pull request page reads `gh pr
            diff`, whose index ids GitHub abbreviates, so each media
            side there reads "Can't show this file here." It shows when
            the diff is too large for GitHub and the page falls back to
            the local merge-base diff. v2 takes an abbreviated id and
            shows the media when the objects are local. Two ways: build
            the PR diff from the local merge-base diff, with
            `--full-index`, whenever the PR's base and head are local,
            and `gh pr diff` only when they are not (where no media can
            show either way, the objects not being local), or look up
            full ids from abbreviated ones on the host. The first is the
            one to take: the lookup reopens the prefix walk the full id
            closes unless it is held to the PR's own diff, which is the
            first way with more steps. Whichever is picked lands as its
            own PR after the first alpha.
            Settled on 2026-10-16, from the owner's "making a pr with
            images seems to error" on alpha.8: the first way, one step
            further. Reproduced in dev builds of `release/v3` 78cf221f4
            and `main` a36aa48bc, both driven with weblab, from a branch
            that adds, changes, renames and deletes png and jpg files
            beside an svg, two binaries that aren't images and a text
            change, with a png pasted into the description. On both, the
            dialog, its body, the push and `gh pr create --attach` went
            through. On v3 the PR's diff page read "Can't show this
            file here." on every image side and never sent a
            `worktrees:readBlob`, while v2 showed every side. That page
            was the error. `getPullRequestDiff`
            (`host/lib/githubCli/actions.ts`) now reads the PR's
            `baseRefOid` and `headRefOid` and answers with `mergeBaseDiff`
            (`--full-index`), media included. A commit that isn't here (a
            head pushed from another device, a base that moved) is
            fetched first, by hash from the remote the PR's URL names,
            the too-large fallback's fetch made the first step. Fetching
            `refs/pull/<n>/head` and the base into `refs/shigomori/pr/<n>/`
            was weighed and not taken: a fetch by hash reaches the same
            commits on GitHub, a fork's head included, and makes no ref,
            so there is nothing to remove when the PR closes. `gh pr
            diff` answers only when the fetch fails, takes over a
            minute or no remote holds the PR, or when git finds no
            merge base for commits it holds (a shallow clone), and then
            a too_large says the commits couldn't be fetched and diffed
            here. `mergeBaseDiff` pins the `a/` and `b/` prefixes,
            which `diff.noprefix` would otherwise drop, and leaves out
            an external diff and textconv, as the hunks read does: the
            patch parser needs git's own patch (the branch diff page
            reads it too). The proof,
            `pull-request-diff`, makes the PR with `createPullRequest`
            from a real repository whose github.com ssh remote a
            `core.sshCommand` serves from a bare repo. It reads the PR's
            diff under `diff.noprefix`, an external diff and a png
            textconv, every blob id one the blob
            read's contract takes and every image side back through
            `readGitBlob`, fetches a PR pushed from another clone with
            no ref made, and falls back to gh. Against the old `getPullRequestDiff`
            all three cases fail. Checked in the dev app: PRs #1 and #3
            of a scratch repo show both sides of every image.
            Then every other diff page's patch got the same pins
            (`PATCH_CONFIG` and `PATCH_FLAGS` in
            `packages/engine/src/git/documents.ts`, read by `fileDiff`,
            `commitDiff`, `mergeBaseDiff` and `readStashDiff`), so a
            user's diff config can't break the working tree's, a
            commit's, a branch's or a stash's page either. The prefixes
            went from flags to config passed before the command
            (`diff.noprefix`, `diff.mnemonicPrefix`, `diff.srcPrefix`
            and `diff.dstPrefix`), because `git stash show` drops
            `--src-prefix` under `diff.noprefix`, and `--default-prefix`
            needs git 2.41, above the 2.40 floor, where an unknown flag
            would fail every patch while an unknown key is ignored.
            `--no-ext-diff --no-textconv` follow the command, as in the
            hunks read. `diff-media` reads all five patches (an edit,
            an untracked file, a commit, a branch, a stash) under
            `diff.noprefix`, `diff.external` and a png textconv. Each
            must parse to its image with both blobs named, and it fails
            for the commit's and the stash's with their config taken
            out. The ui fixtures' copies of the host's arguments carry
            the same pins.
            The first prerelease, `v3.0.0-alpha.1`, was cut on
            2026-10-14 at `release/v3` cd021da02 (#905 on top of the
            #902 to #904 port), with the dev hub deployed from the same
            commit at floor 14 first. Release run 38579892406 built the
            v3 flavor, Shigoto no Mori (v3) against
            `hub-dev.shigomori.com`, signed, notarized and stapled, and
            uploaded the dmg and the zip.
            The second, `v3.0.0-alpha.2`, was cut on 2026-10-14 at
            `release/v3` d3e96a6da (#908, protocol 15, on top of #907),
            and the dev hub deployed from the same commit at floor 15
            only once its assets were up, so no installed alpha.1 was
            turned away before there was a build to update to. Release
            run 38594965018 failed its first attempt in "Build
            distributables" (pnpm's install under the forge build could
            not symlink `typescript` for `packages/engine`, "Directory
            not empty"), and the rerun of that job built, signed,
            notarized and uploaded the dmg and the zip. Checked live
            after the deploy: a protocol 14 request gets the 403
            `HubUpdateRequiredError` naming floor 15, a 15 request the
            401 `HubCredentialRejectedError`.
            The third, `v3.0.0-alpha.3`, was cut on 2026-10-14 at
            `release/v3` 4ebf57dcc (#911, the store driver, on top of
            #910), with no protocol change, so the dev hub stayed at 15.
            Release run 38608832174 failed its first attempt in "Build
            distributables" as alpha.2's had ("Failed to symlink
            "lucide-react" for importer "packages/ui" … Directory not
            empty"), and the rerun built, signed, notarized, ran both
            smokes and uploaded the dmg and the zip. Two first attempts
            in a row made it an item, not a flake: the version step
            edited `app/package.json` after the install, so forge's
            `pnpm exec` installed the whole workspace a second time over
            the hoisted tree the first had laid, and that second install
            sometimes met a folder where it makes a link. The version is
            set before the install now (release.yml).
            The fourth, `v3.0.0-alpha.4`, was cut on 2026-10-14 at
            `release/v3` 48d547acc (#914, a fresh install opening on the
            empty home, on top of #912 and #913), with no protocol
            change, so the dev hub stayed at 15. Release run
            38633662695 was green on its first attempt, the first since
            the version moved before the install: it built, signed,
            notarized, ran both smokes and uploaded the dmg and the zip.
            The terminal command read `--version` 3.0.0-alpha.4 and
            `--json projects list` an empty store, and `update --check`
            answered that GitHub was rate-limiting the runner's address.
            The app opened its store on a data folder that did not
            exist in 3486 ms and on an empty `store.db` in 1514 ms, beside
            the runner's own "Unable to set login item" and plaintext
            hub credential lines.
            The owner found the first alpha.4 bug on a commit's diff
            page: past the rows the first render drew, every file row
            kept its height (pierre's metrics placed it right) and drew
            no lines, an empty box under each header. The React
            Compiler bailed out of `DiffPage` ("Cannot access refs
            during render": the file index was built by a function
            called in render with `selectFile`, a closure over
            `scrollRef`), so its inline `listRef` was a new function on
            every render. React called the old one with null, whose
            `virtualizer.cleanUp()` disconnected every row, and the new
            one's `setup()` had nothing queued to connect again, so after
            the first re-render (the scroll spy's, on the first scroll)
            no row outside the first window ever drew. Main's `DiffView`
            compiled, which hid the same inline ref. Not #905's
            `--full-index` (pierre parses the full ids), not #901's
            media slot, not the metrics. The ref is now made once with
            the virtualizer, so it holds with the compiler or without,
            and the index is JSX, so `DiffPage` compiles again and its
            rows keep stable props. The bailout itself was silent, so
            `pnpm run lint` now runs `app/scripts/react-compiler-check.mts`:
            the compiler over `app/renderer` and `packages/ui/src` with
            its logger, failing on a function it skips with the file,
            function and reason, held to `react-compiler-bailouts.json`,
            the 28 skipped on `release/v3` when it came, each with its
            reason, to be fixed and taken off (a listed one that compiles
            again fails until it is). lefthook runs it on staged renderer
            and ui files. The proof, `commit-diff-rows`, makes a
            repository with git, reads its commit with the host's
            `commitDiff`, serves it through the lab's fake host
            (`window.fakeHost.setPatch`), and scrolls the page in Chrome
            from top to bottom, failing on a row in view with a stretch
            nothing drew or a line outside its row. Against the old
            `DiffPage` it fails on an empty row. The fixtures' patches
            were written by hand and had drifted from anything git
            prints (FAKE_DIFF's hunks declared more lines than they held,
            which pierre logged as "hunk line count mismatch", and short
            ids where the host prints full ones). They are git's output
            now, for repositories built in `packages/ui/test/lib/fixtureRepos.ts`
            and read with the host's arguments, written to
            `packages/ui/src/fixtures/patches.ts`, and the ui suite's
            `fixturePatches` fails when the module and git disagree.
            The fake host reproduced it with a real commit's patch, so
            the dev app and a packaged build were not needed to see it,
            and the fix was checked in the fake host, with the compiler
            on and forced off for `DiffPage`, in both themes.
            Protocol 15 (#908): the six calls the views series replaced
            are gone, `worktrees:fileDiff`, `worktrees:fileHunks`,
            `worktrees:operation`, `worktrees:branchHistory`,
            `worktrees:stashes` and `githubCli:projectPullRequests`, a
            removal an older build would call and get a defect for. Their
            reads live in the views (`watchFileDiff` and the others), and
            the fake host poses each view by its own channel. The dev hub
            deploys at floor 15 with it.
      - [x] Performance and power efficiency, low priority, after the
            others: the numbers already in Measurements, page loads and
            the big transfers against v2 on the same data, and idle
            cost (CPU wake-ups and energy impact of the host, a window,
            a web tab and the daemon while nothing happens), with a fix
            only where a number says so. Evidence: the numbers, in
            Measurements.
            Measured on release/v3 3315bd9c7 against origin/main
            7d2c56daf, dev builds on fresh profiles of one repo
            ("The performance pass" in Measurements). Fixed (#865): the
            store's 500 ms poll, now a watch on its write-ahead log with a
            look 10 s apart for a commit no event announced, which took
            the host's idle wake-ups from 2.3 to 0.4 a second; the host's
            trace log, 100 MB a day at rest and rotated only at start, now
            rotated past 1 MB as it runs; the changes list's checkboxes,
            whose label search per row and per render took a list of 1,935
            files from 2.4 s in v2 to 5.3 s, now 2.6 s in a dev build and
            1.1 s packaged; and a web tab behind another app's window,
            which drew the wallpaper's drift every frame (4.4% of a core
            and 3.6% more in the GPU), now paused as a window is.
            Left, each with its number there: the focused window's
            animations (the design's), the ports view's 5 s probe while
            hidden, file-sync's 2 s parent poll, the history's 323 ms
            packaged against v2's 231 in a dev build, the first paint,
            and Clerk in every window's first chunk. The sends against v2
            could not run: v2 pairs devices through the hub, which the dev
            hub refuses it.
      - Before the first prerelease, the cleanup the net-result
        analysis (2026-10-12) named:
        - [x] ⌃K and ⌃↩ as twins of ⌘K and ⌘↩ on macOS, as v2 had them
              (#877, #882).
        - [x] The two Effect lint rules step 0 left for later, and every
              double cast and suppression reviewed (#880, #884;
              Measurements).
        - [x] The stale docs, and decision 15's outcome (#885).
        - [x] The terminal menu's first key under load on CI (#888).
        - [x] A soak on the tip for every client but the web one
              (Measurements).
      - Before the first prerelease. What the risk map (2026-10-12) found
        that the code can settle now, each for a thread of its own:
        - [x] The move into `wt/` runs at the first start of whatever
              opens the engine, and that includes `sm agents event`,
              which every Claude Code and Codex hook runs, and `sm run`
              (`packages/cli/src/main.ts`, `engine.ts`). After the update
              swaps the bundle, the next hook of any running agent moves
              every worktree out from under that agent, its shells and
              its dev servers, and the app then owes nothing, so the
              migration's page and its device-key step never show. The
              terminal's verbs leave the move to the app, or the move
              leaves a worktree with a process in it where it is, as a
              rename does; a proof runs `sm agents event` on a 2.x
              sandbox and reads what moved.
              Both: only the app's start and `sm doctor --fix` migrate.
              The terminal's other verbs read a 2.x data dir as v2 left
              it, in memory: a command that changes a worktree on disk
              is refused before it starts and a write to the store
              fails, both with `store-unmigrated`; `agents event` and
              the session a command binds do nothing. The move
              leaves a worktree that a process works in (lsof's cwd),
              its stuck line naming each (`close zsh (4123), which runs
              in it`), and one lsof couldn't list stays too. The page's
              Retry moves it once it is free (`WtFolder.retryStuck`),
              and says when it is still stuck; the health check's fix
              does too.
              Held by the terminal's "the v3 migration" cases and
              `WtFolder.test.ts`. Left: until it migrates, the v2
              worktrees read as external, since `wt/` is the managed
              root.
        - [x] The move's port-pool leases: each moved worktree's lease is
              released and made again at its new path, and a failure is
              only logged (`worktrees/moving.ts`). A `WtFolder` case with
              the port pool on holds that the lease follows the move.
              Held by `WtFolder.test.ts` over a stand-in port-pool. A
              lease that didn't follow is the doctor's now: a managed
              worktree port-pool covers (its config in the checkout) with
              no lease in `port-pool list` is `project-leases`, whose fix
              runs `port-pool ensure` on each, so the health check says it
              where the move only logged it.
        - [x] The grants that disagree with their siblings:
              `worktreeData:write` is under changeApp where the other
              title and description edits (`worktreeData:describe`,
              `worktrees:describe`, `worktrees:rename`) are under
              changeCode, and `terminals:list` is gated while
              `scripts:list` and `scripts:watch`, which also name what
              runs in a worktree, are not. Each pair says the same, and
              the classification's diff shows it.
              `worktreeData:write` is under changeCode beside them now,
              and `scripts:list` and `scripts:watch` are gated under
              runCommands, as `terminals:list` is (the owner agreed: a
              peer with the command switch off sees no runs of this
              device, on its Live page or a worktree's script buttons).
        - [x] The agent sessions: `registry.json`'s `agentSessions` is
              imported, re-keyed with the move, so a worktree's agent
              chip and the idle shelf read its running agents at the
              first start.
              The store's agent sessions migration takes them, an entry
              that doesn't parse skipped, and the move re-keys them with
              their worktree (`WtFolder.test.ts`). The migration was
              edited in place, since no build has shipped it.
        - [x] The v2 files go once the import is verified (the owner's
              decision, in place of leaving them until the release after
              3.0.0). The start that made the import reads the files again
              into memory: when nothing is skipped and every table holds
              the same rows as the store, it deletes `registry.json`,
              `config.json`, `state.json`, `running-scripts.json`, the
              mirrors' three under `file-sync/` and the project and
              worktree files, with the folders they empty
              (`src/v2Files.ts`); on any doubt every one stays. A doubt
              is anything the import does without: an entry that doesn't
              parse or holds a key it doesn't model, at any depth, in any
              of the files; a container of the wrong shape; a second
              entry under one id; a key of a file it doesn't read (but
              the files' version, the agent-working marks and an older
              2.x's sidebar view, left out on purpose). The store records
              the hash of each file a verified import deleted
              (`v2_import`). The migration's page says only that the
              import is done. `sm doctor` reports the files left over
              (`v2-files`); its fix deletes only a file whose hash is the
              verified one (a deletion that failed), and says to check
              any other (unverified, or written by v2 since) by hand.
              The files are hashed before and after the verification and
              kept on any change, and the doctor's fix hashes each again
              as it runs. Accepted, by the owner: a v2 write between the
              last hash and the deletion is lost, since v2 takes no lock;
              v2 and v3 are not meant to run on one data folder at once. Held by
              `Store.test.ts` and `Doctor.test.ts`; on a copy of the
              owner's data dir the import verified and deleted every
              file it read.
        - [x] The scripts a 2.x crash left running: the first start
              sweeps the processes `running-scripts.json` names once, by
              the same rule as the orphan sweep (the recorded command on
              a surviving group leader), so a dev server v2 left holds
              no port in v3.
              The running-scripts migration takes the file's scripts as
              rows of the v2 app that wrote it, whose pid is dead by the
              first v3 start, so the sweep stops them and drops the rows
              (`pnpm test orphan-sweep`). The migration was edited in
              place, since no build has shipped it. The store now hands
              the migrations that read files only the platform's
              services: they ran on whatever context the store was
              opened in, so a store opened under another engine's
              (the proof's) wrote its migrations to that one.
        - [x] The import over real data, read-only: a copy of the owner's
              `~/.sm` without `worktrees/` and `updates/`, taken at the same
              moment as the installed v2 `sm`'s `--json list --all` and
              `projects list`, imported alone (no `wt/` move) on a
              throwaway data dir and listed by a v3 `sm` built from this
              branch. The import verified, so its files would be deleted.
              `projects list`: 26 rows in the same order, alike but `hue`,
              null in v3 by decision 12. `list`: 36 rows in the same order,
              alike but the first-parent additions and deletions of three
              merge commits (#867), and 9 worktrees under the v2 root read
              as external, since the move didn't run, so their titles,
              descriptions, agent sessions and one shelf mark don't show;
              the store held all of those as v2 listed them, under the ids
              the move re-keys. No bug. The copy was deleted after.
      - On the signed prerelease. The owner's dogfood of the first
        `v3.0.0-alpha.N`, the steps only a signed and installed build or a
        second machine can take:
        The dogfood started on 2026-10-14 with `v3.0.0-alpha.4`, on the
        owner's Mac, beside v2, whose app, `sm`, data dir, login item and
